No seed or joint-region survivor is asserted.", "evidence": "# C88: complete elimination of the curved partial-tail chronology\n\nStatus: a complete, self-contained analytical proof DRAFT, NOT a verified fact.\nThis is the single recovery round authorized by TASK.md and shared guidance\n6c702a3eacae802d on 2026-09-30. Numerical/model/word discovery and fact_submit\nremain closed. No computation of a candidate word is used in this proof.\n\nThe result below resolves the new family proposed in 657b4a00036ec84f. In\nfact, it allows every lower clipping parameter that preserves its chronology,\nnot just the parameter that puts the third query at the lower ramp boundary.\nThe entire family adds NO constraint to the enlarged unit-curvature system.\nThis is a redundancy result for an explicitly specified family, not a\nstatement about all one-dimensional functions or the original seed cone.\n\n## 1. Statement, canonical variables, and scope\n\nWrite the actual four steps as\n\n    h=(u,v,w,z)=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),\n    H=u+v, R=w+z, s=H+R, C=1+s, P=u+1.\n\nFor this proof assume the following deliberately coarse open box:\n\n    1<u,z<9/5, 1<v,w<5/2, C<9.                       (1)\n\nThis contains the canonical box specified in the complete evidence of\n81720922d2d132d7 and used in 7cd0bb4a795ee1df:\n\n    193/25<=C<87/10,\n    0<a,d<77/97,\n    0<b,c<1552559/1081453,\n    C=5+a+b+c+d.                                     (2)\n\nIndeed 77/97<4/5 and 1552559/1081453<3/2, by direct cross multiplication.\nThe proof here takes (1) as an explicit hypothesis; it does not promote the\nunverified compact-domain derivation to a fact. Thus it applies, without\nany dependency on that derivation, to every actual parameter in the joint\ncanonical region (2), whatever further constraints are imposed there.\nIn particular C is variable; it is never fixed to 193/25.\n\nFor reciprocal curvature t and lower clipping magnitude alpha, let\n\n    1<=t<v, B=v-t>0,\n    0<=alpha<=alpha_B(t):=B/(w+t),\n    f_(t,alpha)(x)=x-t/2                   if x>=t,\n                  x^2/(2t)               if -t*alpha<=x<=t,\n                  -alpha*x-t*alpha^2/2   if x<=-t*alpha.\n                                                               (3)\n\nThe formulas agree at both boundaries. Start actual gradient descent at\nx0=u+t and use the original four steps h. The curvature on the middle\nsegment is k=1/t, strictly between 1/v and 1 inclusive. This is the whole\npartial-tail chronology: the first two gradients are the upper cap, and\nthe next two are the lower cap. The terminal gradient may leave that cap.\nAt the endpoint alpha=alpha_B, its expression in curvature k is\n(k*v-1)/(1+k*w), exactly the parameter in plan 657b4a00036ec84f.\n\nLet V be the ORIGINAL primitive energy of this actual trajectory:\nwrite f_i=f_(t,alpha)(x_i) and g_i=f'_(t,alpha)(x_i), for 0<=i<=4.\n\n    V=2 sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(f_i-f4)\n       +sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+1)g4^2.       (4)\n\nThen the following are equivalent on (1):\n\n    (i) V>=0 for every t and alpha in (3);\n\n    (ii) C(v-1) <= (u+1)(1+w+z),\n         C(v-1)(z-1) <= (u+1)(1+w).                  (5)\n\nIn canonical variables these are exactly\n\n    C b <= (2+a)(3+c+d),\n    C b d <= (2+a)(2+c).                             (6)\n\nBoth are already in the enlarged unit-curvature system. Thus adding ALL\nthe genuine curvatures and ALL the lower clips in this chronology leaves\nthat entire joint region unchanged. This statement is not just an\nequivalent quantified reformulation: the two continuous parameters have\nbeen removed, and (6) is an already present finite list.\n\nNo order between u,v,w,z is assumed. There is no cut saturation,\nquadratic saturation, primitive-prefix assumption, symmetry, or fixed\ncapacity. In particular no claim of nonemptiness of the enlarged joint\nregion is made.\n\n## 2. One static legal function and every interpolation pair\n\nThe derivative of (3) is clamp(x/t,[-alpha,1]). It is continuous,\nnondecreasing, and 1/t-Lipschitz, hence f is convex, differentiable, and\n1-smooth. For alpha>0 its unique minimizer is 0, with value 0. At alpha=0\nits minimizer set is the negative half-line including 0, which is allowed.\nThe starting point x0=u+t is positive in either case. No jets from\ndifferent functions are combined.\n\nThe actual queries, gradients, and terminal coordinate are\n\n    x0=u+t,       g0=1,\n    x1=t,         g1=1,\n    x2=-B,        g2=-alpha,\n    x3=-B+w*alpha,g3=-alpha,\n    x4=-B+R*alpha,\n    g4=clamp(x4/t,[-alpha,1]).                        (7)\n\nTo justify both lower-cap claims, alpha<=B/(w+t) gives\nB>=(w+t)alpha>=t alpha, so x2<=-t alpha. It also gives\nx3=-B+w alpha<=-t alpha. Every displayed update is therefore exactly\nx_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i for the single function (3).\n\nAt every sample, including a clipping boundary,\n\n    f_i=x_i*g_i-t*g_i^2/2.                           (8)\n\nFor a direct audit of ALL interpolation pairs, put n_i=x_i-t*g_i.\nIf g_i is the upper endpoint 1, then n_i>=0; if it is the lower\nendpoint -alpha, then n_i<=0; otherwise n_i=0. Thus, for any two\nsample indices i,j,\n\n    n_j*(g_i-g_j)<=0.\n\nThe original signed residual consequently satisfies\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2\n        =n_j*(g_i-g_j)-(t-1)*(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.        (9)\n\nThis includes every distinct ordered pair in {0,1,2,3,4}, and also every\npair involving the optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0, for which n_star=0.\nWhen alpha=0 the normal assertion still holds: the lower endpoint is 0\nand its normal is nonpositive. No formal coordinate or multiplier is\ndeleted merely because some actual gradients in (7) coincide.\n\nFor interpretation only, accepted fact 04219554e41d9750, Section 10,\ngives the following full original-space identity. Let e be the five-entry\nall-ones column, T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i and zero for t>i, and retain\nall twenty Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j, with Lambda_ii=0. Put\n\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2u,-2v,-2w,-2z,2s)^T,\n    D_prim=diag(u(2-u),v(2-v),w(2-w),z(2-z),-s(s+2)),\n    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow*T+T^T*L_flow^T)/2.\n\nIf L_flow^T e=omega, then\n\n    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g0,...,g4)>.\n                                                               (10)\n\nHere Gram contains all five formal gradients. A full PSD certificate\ntherefore implies V>=0 on (7). Conversely a negative V from this family\nrules out that full certificate. No converse from V>=0 to a certificate\nis asserted. Formula (9) proves actual all-pair legality directly, in the\nsame orientation as accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 3. Exact energy, retaining the curvature deficit\n\nTelescoping x_i^2-x_(i+1)^2=2h_i*x_i*g_i-h_i^2*g_i^2,\nusing (8), gives\n\n    2sum_(i<4)h_i f_i+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2\n       =x0^2-x4^2-(t-1)sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i^2.\n\nSubstitute the actual f4 from (8) into (4), and complete the square:\n\n    V=x0^2-(x4+s*g4)^2\n       -(t-1)[sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i^2-s*g4^2].            (11)\n\nThe bracketed term cannot be discarded by pretending the values have\nunit-curvature saturation. In (7), sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i^2=H+R*alpha^2.\nDefine\n\n    E0(t)=(u+t)^2-(t-1)H,\n    q(t)=1+s/t+s*C/t^2,\n    D(t)=t^2+s*t+s*C=t^2*q(t).\n\nUseful elementary identities are\n\n    E0(1)=P^2,\n    E0(t)=P^2+(t-1)(t+1+u-v),\n    E0'(t)=2t+u-v>1/2 for 1<=t<=v,\n    q(1)=C^2, q'(t)=-s/t^2-2s*C/t^3<0.             (12)\n\nThus E0(t)>=P^2>4, with strict increase for t>1.\n\n## 4. Eliminate every terminal clipping branch and alpha\n\nFirst the terminal upper cap is IMPOSSIBLE on the stated domain; it is\nnot suppressed as an assumption. As alpha increases, x4=-B+R alpha\nincreases. Its maximum is reached at alpha_B, where\n\n    x4=alpha_B*(z-t).\n\nIf z<=t it is nonpositive. If z>t, then\n\n    x4/t <= alpha_B*(z-1)\n          <=[(v-1)/(w+1)]*(z-1)\n          <(3/4)*(4/5)=3/5<1.                       (13)\n\nConsequently no value of alpha in (3), at any curvature, can reach the\nupper terminal cap or its boundary. For completeness, without this box\nthe upper-branch formula from (11) would be\n\n    V=E0(t)-(x4+s)^2-(t-1)R alpha^2+(t-1)s,\n\nbut its domain x4>=t has empty intersection with (3) under (1).\n\nThe lower/core terminal boundary is the positive number\n\n    alpha_A(t)=B/(R+t),\n    0<alpha_A<alpha_B,                               (14)\n\nbecause R=w+z>w. On 0<=alpha<=alpha_A, x4<=-t alpha and\ng4=-alpha. Formula (11) becomes\n\n    V=E0(t)-(B+H alpha)^2+(t-1)H alpha^2\n      =E0(t)-B^2-2BH alpha-H(H+1-t)alpha^2.          (15)\n\nSince H+1-t=u+1+B>0 and B,H>0, (15) strictly decreases with alpha.\nIts minimum on this whole branch is at alpha_A. At alpha=0 it is\n\n    V=H*(u+1+t-v)>0,\n\nbecause u+1+t-v>=u+2-v>1/2.\n\nOn alpha_A<=alpha<=alpha_B, the terminal gradient is x4/t and\n\n    V=E0(t)-(t-1)R alpha^2-q(t)(R alpha-B)^2.         (16)\n\nThis is a concave quadratic in alpha, since q(t)>0 and R>0.\nFor any point of the interval the defining concavity inequality bounds\nits value below by the linear interpolation of its endpoint values;\ntherefore its minimum is at one of the two endpoints. Formulas (15)\nand (16) agree at alpha_A because g4=-alpha_A there. They also remain\nvalid at alpha_B, where x3=-t alpha_B is exactly the boundary between\nthe lower flat segment and the middle ramp.\n\nWe have now proved, without any division by an energy or a residual,\nthat at each fixed t the WHOLE clipping interval is nonnegative if\nand only if both endpoint energies V_A(t), V_B(t) are nonnegative.\n\n## 5. Eliminate curvature at the full-tail endpoint\n\nAt alpha=alpha_A, x4=-t alpha_A and g4=-alpha_A. Formula (11) gives\n\n    V_A(t)=E0(t)-[(t+s)alpha_A]^2\n                     +(t-1)H alpha_A^2.            (17)\n\nThe nonnegative quantity in square brackets is\n\n    J(t)=(v-t)(t+s)/(R+t)\n        =(v-t)*(1+H/(R+t)).\n\nIt decreases on [1,v], because\n\n    J'(t)=-(1+H/(R+t))-H(v-t)/(R+t)^2<0.\n\nUsing (12) and the nonnegative last term in (17) yields\n\n    V_A(t)>=V_A(1)\n          =P^2-[C(v-1)/(R+1)]^2.                   (18)\n\nThe first inequality in (5) is therefore necessary and sufficient for\nevery full-tail endpoint curvature. For t>1, E0(t)>E0(1) shows strict\npositivity whenever V_A(1)>=0. No small-curvature approximation is used.\n\n## 6. Eliminate curvature at the partial-tail return endpoint\n\nAt alpha=alpha_B write alpha(t)=(v-t)/(w+t). Then\n\n    x3=-t alpha(t), x4=(z-t)alpha(t),\n    g4=(z-t)alpha(t)/t,\n    V_B(t)=E0(t)-(t-1)R alpha(t)^2\n                    -q(t)alpha(t)^2(z-t)^2.         (19)\n\nAt t=1 this is\n\n    V_B(1)=P^2-[C(v-1)(z-1)/(w+1)]^2.               (20)\n\nThus necessity of the second inequality in (5) follows by an actual\nunit-curvature member, not by a limiting construction.\n\nFor sufficiency suppose V_B(1)>=0. We must keep BOTH signs of z-t.\n\n### 6a. Return to the positive ray: 1<=t<=z\n\nPut d=z-1 in (0,4/5) and r=t-1 in [0,d], also retaining t<v.\nThe ratio alpha(t) decreases with t and q(t)<=q(1)=C^2. Therefore\n\n    q(t)alpha(t)^2(z-t)^2\n       <=[P^2-V_B(1)]*(d-r)^2/d^2.\n\nUsing the exact E0 in (12), (19) consequently gives\n\n    V_B(t)>=V_B(1)*(d-r)^2/d^2\n       +r*[2+u-v+r+P^2*(2/d-r/d^2)-R alpha(t)^2].   (21)\n\nThe bracket is uniformly positive over the entire box (1). Explicitly,\n\n    2+u-v+r>1/2,\n    P^2*(2/d-r/d^2)>=P^2/d>5,\n    R alpha(t)^2<(9/2)*(3/4)^2=81/32.\n\nThe first estimate uses u>1,v<5/2. The second uses r<=d,\nP>2,d<4/5. The third uses R=w+z<9/2 and\nalpha(t)<=alpha(1)<3/4. Hence the bracket exceeds 95/32. In particular,\n\n    V_B(t)>=V_B(1)*((z-t)/(z-1))^2\n                        +(95/32)*(t-1),            (22)\n\nwith strict inequality in the added estimate when t>1. It follows\nthat every t in this branch has nonnegative energy, strictly positive\nwhen t>1. This includes t=z if that endpoint lies below v.\n\n### 6b. Terminal point on the negative ray: z<=t<v\n\nThis branch has a direct positive margin, independent of (20).\nPut r=t-1. Then 0<=r<v-1<3/2, and\n\n    |g4|=alpha(t)*(t-z)/t\n         <=r*(3/2-r)/[(1+r)(2+r)]<1/6.             (23)\n\nFor the strict last inequality, subtract the numerator after\ncross multiplication:\n\n    (1+r)(2+r)-6r*(3/2-r)\n       =7r^2-6r+2=7(r-3/7)^2+5/7>0.\n\nMoreover\n\n    (t-1)alpha(t)^2\n       <=r*(3/2-r)^2/4<=1/8.                       (24)\n\nThe last scalar bound follows by differentiating\nr*(3/2-r)^2 on [0,3/2]: its only interior maximum occurs at r=1/2\nand has value 1/2. This is a fixed symbolic inequality, not a schedule\nor parameter scan.\n\nSince t<5/2, s<8, and C<9,\n\n    D(t)=t^2+s*t+s*C<25/4+20+72=393/4.\n\nEquation (19), written as\nV_B=E0-(t-1)R alpha^2-D(t)g4^2, together with (12), (23), and (24),\ntherefore proves the uniform bound\n\n    V_B(t)>4-9/16-131/48=17/24>0.                  (25)\n\nThis covers every order between v and z: if z>=v the branch is empty;\nif z<v it covers the entire remaining curvature interval.\n\nSections 6a and 6b exhaust [1,v). Thus (20)>=0 is sufficient for ALL\npartial-tail endpoint curvatures. Together with Sections 4 and 5, this\nproves the equivalence (5) for the WHOLE two-parameter family.\n\n## 7. Boundary, zero-credit, and denominator audit\n\n* t=1 is included exactly. The curvature-deficit term in (11) is then\n  zero, but no step divides by t-1. Both constraints in (5) are attained\n  by positive finite alpha values at t=1.\n* alpha=0 is a valid static function with a nonunique minimizer. Its\n  energy is the positive value given after (15); it is not discarded.\n* At alpha_A the lower and middle formulas agree. At alpha_B query3\n  lies exactly on the lower ramp boundary. Both are actual trajectories.\n* Terminal upper clipping and its equality boundary were excluded by\n  the strict bound (13), not by an unproved cap assumption. Lower\n  terminal clipping was fully retained in (15).\n* At t=z the terminal gradient is zero. Both (22) and (25) apply if\n  t=z<v, and neither divides by g4 or z-t.\n* The closure t=v forces B=alpha_A=alpha_B=alpha=0. The limiting actual\n  trajectory is x=(u+v,v,0,0,0), g=(1,1,0,0,0), with curvature 1/v\n  and V=H*(u+1)>0. The same formulas remain valid. Curvatures t>v\n  cannot put query2 on the negative flat part with alpha>0; they are\n  outside this specified chronology, not an omitted branch of it.\n* All denominators t,w+t,R+t,w+1,R+1 are strictly positive. The only\n  division by d=z-1 occurs in the positive-return branch with d>0.\n  On the optional boundary z=1 that branch has only t=1; (19) at t=1\n  is P^2, and the negative-ray argument handles t>1. No zero-denominator\n  case is silently assigned a limit value in the proof.\n* There is no cut-repair weight or division by an interpolation credit\n  anywhere in this family. Vanishing residuals, repeated actual gradients,\n  and zero curvature-deficit t-1 cause no singularity.\n* Equality in either unit constraint is allowed. Its endpoint may have\n  V=0 at t=1; every member with t>1 has strictly positive endpoint\n  energies and hence positive energy throughout the clipping interval.\n\n## 8. Direct screening, consequence, and remaining full interface\n\nThe direct route succeeds: monotonicity of (15), concavity of (16), and\nthe two curvature estimates completely remove alpha and t. The backup\ncubic-stationary-root route is unnecessary. It is not an additional\nresult or a claim of computational discovery.\n\nEvery schedule in the enlarged canonical necessary system already\nsatisfies (6), so every such schedule survives the ENTIRE curved\npartial-tail family (3). This remains true at every unit-constraint\nequality and for every capacity in (2). Thus curvature in the selected\nchronology cannot resolve any surviving joint region. The obstruction\nis concrete: each interior alpha is controlled by two endpoint\nenergies; the full-tail endpoint is weakest at unit curvature; the\npositive return loses terminal energy faster than it incurs the exact\ncurvature deficit; and a negative return has the uniform margin (25).\n\nNo new surviving schedule or interval is asserted. In particular the\nold h(t) interval from 480b207356f9095f is already rejected by the\nunit-return inequality and is not reconsidered here. This proof does\nnot imply that the enlarged necessary region is nonempty or empty.\n\nThe unresolved seed question remains: does any four-step schedule of\nsum at least 168/25 admit ALL twenty nonnegative multipliers with the\nexact flow and the full five-gradient PSD matrix in (10)? Passing this\nfamily does not provide those multipliers. Accepted 04219554e41d9750\nremains conditional on a seed that has not been constructed. The\noriginal arbitrary-h asymptotic exact-profile problem remains open.\n\nOne genuinely different next geometry would use a two-dimensional\ngradient set with noncollinear exposed faces and an anisotropic\nquadratic conjugate, so the actual gradients on the successive return\nneed not remain parallel. A concrete starting representation is\n\n    F(x)=sup_(g in K) [<x,g>-(1/2)<A g,g>],\n\nwhere K is a compact convex polygon containing 0 and A is a symmetric\nmatrix with A>=I. The actual-query conditions are\nx_i-A g_i in N_K(g_i), together with the original GD recurrences.\nHere I is the identity matrix and\nN_K(g)={n:<n,v-g><=0 for every v in K} is the normal cone.\nThis is a proposed new geometry, not a realized seed, certificate, or\nauthorized numerical search. The scalar endpoint/concavity argument\nabove must not be extended to it without a new proof. Another query\nchronology, in which query3 has left the lower flat segment, is also\noutside (3); no all-curvature claim about that chronology is made.\n\n## 9. Provenance and resource accounting\n\nThis joint proof uses the accepted full-certificate identity\n04219554e41d9750 and the consistent interpolation convention in\n1252b8823bb5a290. Every new function, trajectory, pair residual, energy,\nand parameter inequality used here is derived in this document.\nThe box and unit-constraint mapping are made explicit, so no shared\nunverified claim is used as a hidden premise.\n\nMatlas literature retrieval returned no applicable new result. The\nalready downloaded Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper, arXiv:1502.05666,\nTheorem 4 and its proof, was reread for context. No theorem from it is\nimported; (9) supplies the exact required all-pair calculation.\n\nRound deltas: zero numerical/model/word evaluations, zero solver calls,\nzero numerical replays, zero fact_submit calls, zero installations,\nzero subagents. {"id": "250d197b0a861a30", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T09:28:43.286327+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C90 complete UNVERIFIED joint draft: arbitrary SIGNED correlations inside mutually orthogonal queried subspaces obey V<=A_tail beta_(M-1), where A_tail prices only the last actual consecutive run. Therefore EVERY fixed-cardinality character correlation subgroup, even placed in high bits and with unrestricted signed moments/amplitudes/values/free terminal, has vanishing original-B payoff on original H_m. A new full-support nonproduct law k(1)=-1/2,k(6)=-1/3,k(7)=1/4 has V<=128/N^2 and an explicit common smooth function, ALL original pairs, unique minimizer0 and exact true radius.", "evidence": "# C90: signed correlations in orthogonal components\n\nStatus: **complete joint draft, UNVERIFIED**. This is completed round22,\nafter baseline21, within ceiling23 (the historical interruption is counted\nseparately). No numerical evaluation, scan, replay, solver, installation,\nsubagent, or verifier call was used. Correctness predecessors are accepted\n`1252b8823bb5a290`, `0e7e78056dc6258c`, and `b98bb807d514e80d`.\nEarlier unverified character manuscripts are motivation only. All new\narguments used here are proved together below.\n\nThe original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile question remains open.\n\n## 1. The general signed statement\n\nLet F be a convex differentiable function on a finite-dimensional Euclidean\nspace, with 1-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer. Run actual GD with a\nfinite nonnegative schedule h of length n>=1. Write X_i for its positions,\ng_i=grad F(X_i), f_i=F(X_i)-min F, and\n\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i, R=dist(X_0,argmin F).\n\nSuppose the nonzero QUERIED gradients g_0,...,g_(n-1) belong to M mutually\northogonal subspaces V_1,...,V_M, each of which is used at least once.\nThe gradients within a subspace can have arbitrary signed correlations,\narbitrary norms, and arbitrary linear dependencies. No condition is imposed\non the unqueried terminal gradient. A run is a maximal consecutive interval\nof queried indices having the same subspace label. Let r be the first index\nof the final run, and set\n\n    A_tail=product_(t=r)^(n-1) max{1,(h_t-1)^2}.\n\nThe candidate conclusion is\n\n    2 f_n/R^2 <= A_tail beta_(M-1),                     (1)\n    c_0=0, c_(j+1)=c_j+2+2sqrt(c_j+1),\n    beta_j=4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_j))^2.\n\nIf any queried gradient is zero, the trajectory remains at that minimizer\nand the terminal gap is zero. Thus excluding zero gradients loses no\npositive payoff. In the nonzero case R>0.\n\nThe important difference from C89 is that (1) does NOT assume nonnegative\ncorrelations with earlier gradients. Those terms remain signed; the proof\nchooses intervals on which they vanish by orthogonality of whole subspaces.\n\n## 2. First occurrences: every skipped term is retained\n\nTranslate a closest minimizer to zero. Such a minimizer exists because its\nset is nonempty and closed, and a bounded distance-minimizing sequence has\na convergent subsequence. Accepted `1252b8823bb5a290` gives every original\nordered inequality\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.       (2)\n\nList the first occurrence of each subspace, in chronological order, as\nj_0<...<j_(M-1). Put v_a=g_(j_a), q_a=||v_a||^2, and p_a=f_(j_a).\nThe vectors v_a are pairwise orthogonal. Moreover\n\n    <v_a,g_t>=0 for EVERY t<j_a,                       (3)\n\nbecause none of those earlier gradients belongs to its subspace.\nThere are no compressed GD steps in this argument. Applying the original\nQ_(j_(a+1),j_a) gives exactly\n\n    p_a >= p_(a+1)+(q_a+q_(a+1))/2\n              +sum_(t=j_a)^(j_(a+1)-1)h_t<v_(a+1),g_t>\n        = p_(a+1)+(q_a+q_(a+1))/2.                    (4)\n\nThe original optimum row, retaining its past displacement, gives\n\n    <X_0,v_a> >= p_a+q_a/2\n                       +sum_(t<j_a)h_t<v_a,g_t>\n                 =p_a+q_a/2.                         (5)\n\nAlso p_(M-1)>=q_(M-1)/2. Define\nb=p_(M-1)-q_(M-1)/2>=0 and T_a=b+sum_(s=a)^(M-1)q_s.\nEquations (4)-(5) imply <X_0,v_a>>=T_a>0. Expanding the squared norm\nafter subtracting the orthogonal projections onto v_a yields\n\n    R^2>=sum_a T_a^2/q_a.                              (6)\n\nFor completeness, the scalar elimination in accepted\n`0e7e78056dc6258c` applies as follows. Inductively\nsum_(a<k)T_a^2/q_a>=c_k T_k. Adding the next term and using\nT_k=q_k+T_(k+1) reduces this to\n\n    (c_k+1)q_k+(c_k+2)T_(k+1)+T_(k+1)^2/q_k\n       >=[c_k+2+2sqrt(c_k+1)]T_(k+1),\n\nwhich is the nonnegativity of a square. With c=c_(M-1),\nz=T_(M-1)/q_(M-1)>=1 and u=(1+sqrt(1+2c))/2, we get\nR^2>=q_(M-1)z(z+c), 2p_(M-1)=q_(M-1)(2z-1), and\n\n    z(z+c)-u^2(2z-1)=(z-u)^2>=0.\n\nTherefore 2p_(M-1)/R^2<=beta_(M-1). The same proof includes M=1.\nInduction gives c_k>=k^2 and hence beta_k<=2/k^2 for k>=1.\n\n## 3. Run starts cannot transmit an increase to another subspace\n\nConsider consecutive run starts u<v. All gradients g_t for u<=t<v\nbelong to the old subspace; g_v belongs to a different, orthogonal one.\nThe actual original pair Q_(v,u) therefore says\n\n    f_u>=f_v+||g_u-g_v||^2/2\n                         +sum_(t=u)^(v-1)h_t<g_v,g_t>\n        =f_v+(||g_u||^2+||g_v||^2)/2.                  (7)\n\nThis identity keeps every physical step of the old run. In particular,\neven a large increase INSIDE that run cannot be passed to the next run\nstart. A first occurrence is a run start. Applying (7) successively after\nthe last first occurrence proves f_r<=p_(M-1).\n\nFor any actual step of a 1-smooth convex function, the segment smoothness\nbound and ||g||^2<=2f give\n\n    f(X-hg)<=max{1,(h-1)^2}f(X), h>=0.                 (8)\n\nFor h<=2 this is descent. For h>2, use\nf(X-hg)<=f(X)+h(h-2)||g||^2/2<=(h-1)^2f(X).\nApply (8) to the complete physical suffix beginning at r. This proves (1).\nNo sign assumption has been made within any V_a.\n\n## 4. Consequence for arbitrary signed finite-support character kernels\n\nFix m>=4, N=2^m, n=N-1, and retain the original enclosing parameters\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,\n    c_chr=sigma(1+1/m), a_chr=delta/c_chr,\n    h_i=1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n.             (9)\n\nHere nu_2 is the exponent of 2 dividing a positive integer. These are\nexactly H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_chr*c_chr^j,H_j), with the SAME enclosing\nm in every child. The last physical step is 1+a_chr<2.\n\nLet w_s be any nonnegative probability law on s in {0,1}^m, and define\nchi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_l s_l i_l), k(d)=sum_s w_s chi_s(d). Suppose k(d)=0\noutside a binary subgroup H of cardinality K. Prescribe queried gradients\n\n    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s)chi_s(i))_s, Gamma_i>0.\n\nThen <g_i,g_j>=Gamma_i Gamma_j k(i XOR j). Different H-cosets span\northogonal subspaces; all within-coset correlations may be negative.\nIf K>=2, deleting the one unqueried label N-1 leaves all M=N/K cosets\nrepresented. Each coset has at most K queried labels, so the final run\ncontains at most K physical steps.\n\nFor that final suffix, write its indices as N-1-j with 1<=j<=q<=K.\nThen nu_2(N-j)=nu_2(j). Levels zero and one have offsets a_chr<1 and\ndelta<1. For a level k>=2 the offset delta*c_chr^(k-1) is at most\nc_chr^(k-1). Consequently\n\n    A_tail <= c_chr^(2 sum_(j=1)^K (nu_2(j)-1)_+)\n           = c_chr^(2 sum_(k>=2) floor(K/2^k))\n           <= c_chr^K <= [5sigma/4]^K.                (10)\n\nThe displayed sum is finite and the middle identity just counts, for each\nj, how many powers 2^k with k>=2 divide it. It is NOT an enumeration or a\nnumerical computation. If M>=2, (1) yields\n\n    2f_n/R^2 <= [5sigma/4]^K beta_(N/K-1)\n              <=8[5sigma/4]^K K^2/N^2.               (11)\n\nFor K=1 every queried gradient is orthogonal to every other, so M=n,\nthe final run is one step, and (1) applies directly with beta_(n-1).\n\nThus every fixed-size correlation subgroup fails to supply a nonvanishing\noriginal-B payoff on (9), regardless of where its binary generators lie,\nof any signs in its kernel, of its amplitudes, or of its terminal extension.\nIndeed accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` gives\nB_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3)<2, so multiplying (11) by B_n tends to zero.\nThis also covers high-bit subgroups: moving a fixed support to a high\nbridge is not an escape. The proof prices the actual FINAL run, rather\nthan bounding the entire interval after the last first occurrence.\n\nMore generally (1) holds without characters whenever the queried\ngradients split into orthogonal subspaces with a controlled final run.\nIt does not exclude a dense correlation graph, growing components whose\nactual suffix cost defeats (11), or geometries with correlations between\nthe proposed components.\n\n## 5. A different full-support law with genuine negative history\n\nFor m>=4 define the real signs B=(-1)^s_0, C=(-1)^s_1, D=(-1)^s_2 and\n\n    w_s=N^-1[1-B/2-CD/3+BCD/4].                        (12)\n\nFor (B,CD)=(+,+),(+,-),(-,+),(-,-), the density factors in brackets are\n5/12,7/12,11/12,25/12. Each occurs equally often, their average is one,\nand every atom is positive. The only nonzero nonconstant moments are\n\n    k(1)=-1/2, k(6)=-1/3, k(7)=1/4.                   (13)\n\nAll other moments vanish by pairing a free character bit. Thus H={0,1,6,7}.\nThe moments do not form a product law in any linear sign basis on H:\nnone of -1/2,-1/3,1/4 equals the product of the other two. For example\n1/4-(-1/2)(-1/3)=1/12. This is a different law from C89.\n\nIts negative history is explicit. Every original leaf has correlation\n-1/2; at the first level-two bridge the triple at indices 2,3,4 has\ncorrelations -1/2,-1/3,+1/4 for pairs 23,24,34. A largest-representative\nselection of the kind used in C89 has negative correlations with skipped\nearlier members of its own coset. In particular the last queried gradient\ng_(N-2) has correlation -1/3 with g_(N-8). C89's nonnegative-history\nhypothesis fails; those negative terms cannot be discarded.\n\nIn every eight-label slab the coset labels occur as A,A,B,B,B,B,A,A.\nThe missing terminal label removes the last A of the last slab. Hence the\nfinal queried run contains only index N-2. Its last physical step is less\nthan two, so A_tail=1. Applying the complete argument above gives\n\n    V_sig=2f_n/R^2<=beta_(N/4-1)<=128/N^2,\n    0<=B_n V_sig<=128(1+4n^p)/(n+1)^2 ->0.             (14)\n\nThis is uniform over all positive queried amplitudes, anchors, values,\nfunctions, and free terminal gradients realizing this law.\n\n## 6. Actual nonemptiness, all original pairs, and the true radius\n\nThe following explicit construction applies to (12), including a\nprescribed terminal gradient, on the complete physical word (9).\nIn fact its pair checks work for any finite schedule with every h_i>1.\nDefine\n\n    H_max=max_(i<n)h_i,\n    kappa=min{1, min_(i<n)(h_i-1)/4},\n    eta=kappa/[16(H_max+kappa+1)],\n    Gamma_i=eta^i, f_i=(kappa+1/2)Gamma_i^2, 0<=i<=n,\n    K_ij=<g_i,g_j>.\n\nUse (12) and the character gradients for ALL labels 0,...,n. Set\n\n    b_i=(2kappa+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t K_ti,\n    (X_0)_s=[1/(N sqrt(w_s))]\n                    sum_(i=0)^n(b_i/Gamma_i)chi_s(i),\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.                          (15)\n\nThe elementary character identity sum_s chi_s(i)chi_s(j)=N 1_(i=j)\nfollows by pairing a differing bit. Thus\n<X_0,g_i>=b_i and <X_i,g_i>=(2kappa+1)Gamma_i^2.\n\nFor EVERY original pair i<j, direct substitution gives\n\n    Q_ij=(kappa+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa Gamma_j^2\n                        -sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_it-K_ij,\n    Q_ji=(kappa+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa Gamma_i^2\n                        +(h_i-1)K_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_jt. (16)\n\nNo signed term has been removed from these identities. For estimates use\n|K_it|<=Gamma_i Gamma_t, which follows from the explicit unit character\ndirections and the nonnegative probability weights. Since eta<1/2 and\nh_i>=1+4kappa, the first line divided by Gamma_i^2 is at most\n\n    -3kappa+H_max eta/(1-eta)+eta\n       <=-3kappa+2(H_max+1)eta <=-23kappa/8<0.\n\nWriting d=j-i>=1, the second line divided by Gamma_i^2 is at most\n\n    -kappa+(kappa+1)eta^2+H_max eta/(1-eta)\n       <=-kappa+2(H_max+kappa+1)eta=-7kappa/8<0.\n\nThe original optimum rows are exactly\n\n    Q_(i,star)=Q_(star,i)=-kappa Gamma_i^2<0.           (17)\n\nIn particular the smallest actual nonempty block, the first leaf\nh_0=1+a_chr, has\n\n    Q_01=kappa-a_chr-kappa eta^2+eta/2<0,\n    Q_10=(kappa+1)eta^2-kappa-a_chr eta/2<0.            (18)\n\nThese are its genuine negative correlation terms, not a positive-history\nsurrogate. Equations (16)-(17) simultaneously check every later block,\nboth orientations of every remote pair, and all terminal pairs.\n\nHere is ONE function fixed for the entire run. Put\n\n    y_i=X_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2=kappa Gamma_i^2,\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,max_(0<=i<=n)(<y_i,v>-delta_i)},\n    F_sig(X)=sup_v(<X,v>-H(v)).                        (19)\n\nThe constructive interpolation proof in accepted `1252b8823bb5a290`,\napplied to this whole finite jet set, proves that F_sig is everywhere\nfinite, convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth, and has exactly all the\npositions, gradients, and values (15)-(17). Its maximizer is unique\nbecause H is 1-strongly convex. Thus the same F_sig realizes the original\nGD recurrence for every physical step, without resets or padding.\n\nEvery delta_i is strictly positive. Consequently H(v)=||v||^2/2 in a\nneighborhood of zero. Also H>=||v||^2/2, so F_sig(0)=0. For X!=0, choose\nv=tX in that neighborhood with 0<t<1; then\nF_sig(X)>=(t-t^2/2)||X||^2>0. Hence argmin F_sig={0}.\nThe TRUE radius is therefore exactly\n\n    R^2=||X_0||^2\n       =N^-2 sum_s w_s^-1\n                       [sum_i(b_i/Gamma_i)chi_s(i)]^2. (20)\n\nIt is finite and at least one, since ||g_0||=1 and the gradient is\n1-Lipschitz relative to its unique minimizer. The positive actual payoff is\n\n    2F_sig(X_n)/R^2=(2kappa+1)eta^(2n)/R^2.            (21)\n\nThis construction proves nonemptiness and makes its radius cost explicit;\nit is not claimed to be a competitive amplitude choice. Bound (14) applies\nto every other legal amplitude choice as well.\n\n## 7. Scope, literature, and remaining direction\n\nThe selected route was screened by direct proving. Equations (3)-(7) close\nthe signed component obstruction; (15)-(21) close full interpolation and\nnormalization of an actual signed law. The attempted positive normalized\npayoff for a fixed-size component law is therefore excluded at joint-draft\nlevel. This is not a verifier verdict.\n\nMatlas was queried for negatively correlated GD block constructions and\nstrict finite interpolation; its hits did not supply the missing result.\nAn early web search recovered the finite interpolation literature. The\nexact local text of Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666,\nSection2.5, Theorem4, its proof, construction, Corollary1 and Remark3 was\nread. The needed interpolation result is already proved in accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290, so no external theorem is imported as a premise.\n\nAn actual uncovered freedom is a DENSE signed correlation geometry whose\nquery graph has one large component. Neither a different fixed-size\nsubgroup, higher-order dependence within it, nor moving it to high bits\nescapes (11). A promising concrete next test is to strictify a known scalar\nsigned trajectory and add a small full-support orthogonal perturbation.\nIts common function and radius must be built jointly: a covariance matrix\nalone does not provide either. Such a lift would calibrate the dense law;\nit would not automatically improve the known scalar payoff or supply the\nuniform arbitrary-h target.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max3_c90_signed_subspace_runs", "status": "complete_unverified_joint_draft", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "0e7e78056dc6258c", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["a4cf0eba9d9349e3", "767cf03df8682bf8", "967d09ceaf382413"], "artifact": "workers/max3/research/c90_signed_components_run_obstruction.md", "round": 22}, "glossary": {"V_a": "Mutually orthogonal subspaces containing queried gradients in the C90 joint draft.", "A_tail": "Product of max{1,(h_t-1)^2} over the original physical suffix beginning at the final queried run.", "H_sig": "Binary correlation support subgroup; in the explicit C90 law it is {0,1,6,7}.", "K_sig": "Cardinality of a binary correlation support subgroup, denoted K locally.", "F_sig": "The explicit conjugate maximum-of-quadratics interpolant in C90.", "V_sig": "Twice the terminal gap divided by the squared true initial distance.", "Gamma_i": "Positive prescribed character-gradient amplitude at original sample i.", "H_max": "Largest original physical step in the finite schedule.", "kappa": "Positive strict interpolation parameter min{1,min_i(h_i-1)/4} in C90.", "eta": "Fast-decay amplitude ratio kappa/[16(H_max+kappa+1)].", "b_i": "Prescribed anchor inner product (2kappa+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_{t<i}h_t<g_t,g_i>.", "chi_s": "Real binary character evaluated on an original sample label."}}
{"id": "e59fe188876aa98d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T09:35:22.902243+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "NEW complete JOINT DRAFT: full ordinary primitive certificates are reversal invariant for EVERY nonnegative word with <=2 positive steps. The 3x3 inverse map has four explicitly nonpositive nonterminal-target entries; any remaining bad terminal coefficient is removed by a full-matrix re-selection. Consequently every full four-step certificate with a SATURATED MIDDLE cut admits an explicit reversed full certificate, and that class has capacity<77/10<193/25. All original zero-step gradients are restored. General four-step reversal and the threshold region remain OPEN.", "evidence": "# Full primitive reversal for short blocks and saturated middle cuts\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2's first new\nanalytical round under TASK.md and master_guidance 8211d6128deabdf3\n(completed baseline20, ceiling22). No numerical evaluation, solver, fixed\nreplay, verifier, installation, or subordinate agent is used.\n\nAccepted input: fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11, supplies the exact\nfull primitive interface and the full saturated primitive join identity.\nFact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the smooth-convex interpolation convention.\nAll additional algebra used below is proved here. Earlier shared drafts\n4a064f93111ac5a2, f3d96c901566e96b, and f7cee3034df8cb7f motivated the\ninversion, repair, and cut arguments; none is used as a verified lemma.\n\n## 1. Statements and scope\n\nFor a finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), put s=sum h_i and\nC=1+s. On formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i define\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                              -sC||g_n||^2.\n\nA full ordinary primitive certificate is an array lambda_ij>=0 on ALL\nordered distinct finite query pairs such that V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\ncancels every independent f_i and is PSD on ALL n+1 independent gradients.\nEntries are allowed to be zero; no support is imposed on the input.\nThis is exactly the interface in fact04219554e41d9750. A signed-terminal\ncertificate permits arbitrary signs only on lambda_(i,n), i<n.\n\nThe joint claims below are:\n\n(A) For every nonnegative word with at most two positive steps, existence\nof a full ordinary primitive certificate is equivalent to existence for\nits reversal. The proof gives an explicit certificate transformation,\nincluding a possible change of the PSD remainder and full zero-step lift.\n\n(B) Suppose h=(a,1+r,b), A=1+sum a, D=1+sum b,\nT=A+D+r, r>0, and rT=AD. Suppose each child a,b has at most two positive\nsteps. A full ordinary certificate for h yields full ordinary certificates\nfor BOTH children after re-selection. Consequently h and rev(h) have\nordinary certificates simultaneously, by an explicit construction.\n\n(C) In particular, for four physical steps and a saturated middle cut\n(at index1 or2), ordinary full-certificate feasibility is reversal\ninvariant. Any such feasible word has capacity T<77/10<193/25.\nThus a four-step threshold seed from fact04219554e41d9750 cannot have\neither middle cut saturated.\n\nThese assertions do NOT prove arbitrary four-step reversal. They cannot\ntransport the threshold C82/C83 prism exclusions: the class in(C) does\nnot meet the threshold region. Endpoint saturated cuts can leave a\nthree-positive-step child, which is outside(A)--(B). Fully unsaturated\nfour-step words are also outside(B). The original exact-profile goal\nremains open. No new fact is asserted.\n\n## 2. Zero-step compression and full-space restoration\n\nThis preliminary statement applies to ordinary certificates. It also\napplies to signed-terminal certificates when all nonterminal-targeting\ncoefficients are nonnegative.\n\nGiven a nonnegative word, partition its original query indices into\nclasses that represent the same point after deleting zero updates.\nRestrict a supplied certificate to formal data where the values and\ngradients in each class are equal. Every original position becomes the\ncorresponding compressed formal position. A Q between two different\nclasses is the corresponding compressed Q; a Q within a class vanishes.\nAggregate multipliers over each ordered pair of different classes.\nValue cancellation is preserved. The matrix restriction is a congruence,\nso it preserves PSD. Nonnegative signs are preserved. In the signed\ncase only edges entering the final compressed class can be signed.\nThe original energy restricts to the compressed energy because zero\nsteps have coefficient zero, and the original terminal becomes the\ncompressed terminal.\n\nConversely, for an ORDINARY compressed certificate, choose the original\nindex of each positive step as its query representative, and the original\nindex n as the terminal representative. Put its multiplier on that\nrepresentative ordered pair and set every other original multiplier to\nzero. The represented positions are exactly the compressed GD positions,\nas formal identities, since every omitted update has zero coefficient.\nThe original energy equals the compressed one on those selected formal\ngradients; its unused gradients have coefficient zero. Therefore the\nlifted identity is PSD on the entire original space, with zero rows and\ncolumns at unused gradients. No equality of duplicated formal gradients\nis imposed in the FINAL certificate.\n\nAll-zero and empty words have V_h=0 and the zero certificate. Reversal\ncommutes with positive-step compression up to this representative choice.\nIt therefore suffices to prove(A) for one or two strictly positive steps.\n\n## 3. Complete two-step signed-terminal repair\n\nThis section proves the repair used after inversion and after a cut.\nFor one positive step u, flow gives lambda_10=lambda_01+2u. Evaluation\nat equal gradients gives zero energy and zero Q, so PSD implies that the\nall-one vector is in the matrix kernel. Direct expansion then gives\nlambda_01=2 and the full identity\n\n V_(u)+2Q_01+(2+2u)Q_10=(2-u^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.          (1)\n\nThus u<=sqrt(2), and the coefficients are already ordinary.\n\nFor two positive steps (u,v), s=u+v, introduce coefficient names\n\n a=lambda_01, b=lambda_12, c=lambda_02, d=lambda_20.\n\nOnly b,c are allowed to be signed. The other four entries are\nnonnegative. Value cancellation gives\n\n lambda_10=a+c-d+2u,\n lambda_21=b+c-d+2s.\n\nEqual gradients, with values following an affine trajectory, again give\nV=Q=0; PSD implies the all-one vector is in the kernel. Expanding its\nfirst and last rows gives\n\n u*a+s*c=2u,\n v*(b+c)+u*d=2s(u+1).\n\nConsequently\n\n a=2-(s/u)c,\n b=2s(u+1)/v-c-(u/v)d,\n lambda_10=2(u+1)-(v/u)c-d,\n b=2(u+1)+(u/v)lambda_10>0.                           (2)\n\nTo audit both the kernel equations and the entire matrix, use U=g_0-g_1,\nW=g_1-g_2. Substitution of the six Q terms gives\n\n M=[2-u^2+v(u-1)c/u]||U||^2\n       +[c-(u-1)d]<U,W>+[Cpair-(u/v)d]||W||^2,\n Cpair=s[2(u+1)/v-s].                                (3)\n\nFor example, the forward adjacent Q_01 has quadratic part\n(1/2-u)||g_0||^2-<g_0,g_1>+||g_1||^2/2; its reverse has\n(||g_0||^2+||g_1||^2)/2+(u-1)<g_0,g_1>.\nFor Q_02 use x_2-x_0=-u g_0-v g_1; for Q_20 use its negative.\nThese expansions, the value equations, and(2) give(3) without a\nrestriction on the independent gradients.\n\nIf c>=0, every supplied coefficient is ordinary already, by(2).\nIf c<0, nonnegativity of the W diagonal in(3), with d>=0, gives\nCpair>=0. If u>=1, nonnegativity of the U diagonal and its nonpositive\nc term give u<=sqrt(2). If u<1 that bound holds directly.\nReplace the array by\n\n lambda'_01=2, lambda'_10=2+2u,\n lambda'_12=2s(u+1)/v, lambda'_21=2s(u+1)/v+2s,\n lambda'_02=lambda'_20=0.\n\nThese are nonnegative and satisfy exact flow. Formula(3) now gives\n\n M'=(2-u^2)||U||^2+Cpair||W||^2>=0.                    (4)\n\nThis is a change of the full PSD matrix, not a claim about preserving\nits rank, its range, or its difference in PSD order. Section2 extends\nthis repair to words with at most two positive steps and any zero steps.\n\n## 4. Unsigned reversal identity, rederived on the full space\n\nLet the two positive steps be h=(u,v), C=1+u+v. Let e=(1,1,1)^T,\ne_2=(0,0,1)^T, hbar=(u,v,0)^T, w=(u,v,1)^T, and\n\n T_h=[[1,0,0],[u,1,0],[u,v,1]], p=T_h e=(1,1+u,C)^T.\n\nLet L be the row Laplacian of the supplied ordinary multipliers:\nL_ij=-lambda_ij for i!=j, L_ii=sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij. Its flow is\nL^T e=(-2u,-2v,2(u+v))^T. Define\n\n K=L+diag(hbar)-hbar e_2^T+diag(w).\n\nDirect expansion of the full interface in fact04219554e41d9750 gives\n\n M=sym(K T_h)-C^2 e_2 e_2^T,                         (5)\n K e=w, K^T e=C e_2.\n\nFor an explicit check of(5), sym((K-L)T_h)=diag(u(2-u),v(2-v),1).\nMoreover e^T M e=0, hence M e=0, and therefore\n\n K p=-C w+2C^2 e_2.\n\nAll off-diagonal entries of K are nonpositive. Its row-sum surplus is\nw_i>0 at every row. If Kz=0, a coordinate attaining max_i|z_i| and\nstrict row diagonal dominance force this maximum to be zero. Thus K\nis invertible. Put G=K^(-1). The displayed equations imply\n\n G w=e, G e_2=(p+C e)/(2C^2), e_2^T G=e^T/C.           (6)\n\nDefine\n\n Delta=[[0,-1,1],[-1,1,0],[1,0,0]].\n\nFor hrev=(v,u), write wrev=(v,u,1)^T and T_rev=T_(v,u).\nDirect multiplication gives\n\n Delta e=e_2, Delta p=wrev, Delta T_rev=T_h^T Delta.\n\nSet F=Delta G Delta and\n\n K_rev=4C^2 F-(wrev+2C e_2)e_2^T,\n lambda_rev,ij=-4C^2 F_ij  (i!=j).                    (7)\n\nFor now these coefficients are allowed to be signed. Equations(6) give\nK_rev e=wrev and K_rev^T e=C e_2. The off-diagonals in(7), including\nits last-column correction, are exactly the grounding of lambda_rev;\nthe row sums fix its diagonals. Thus every reversed function-value\ncoefficient cancels with the required flow (-2v,-2u,2(u+v)).\n\nThe entire reversed quadratic remainder obeys\n\n M_rev=4C^2 Delta G M G^T Delta.                       (8)\n\nHere is a full algebra check. Inserting K_rev into sym(K_rev T_rev)\nproduces the non-boundary term 4C^2 sym(Delta G T_h^T Delta).\nAfter subtracting C^2 e_2 e_2^T, its boundary term is\n\n -(wrev+C e_2)(wrev+C e_2)^T.\n\nInserting(5) in the right side of(8) produces the same non-boundary\nterm and the same boundary term, since Delta G e_2=(wrev+C e_2)/(2C^2).\nThis proves(8) on all three independent reversed gradients. No\npositivity of the output multipliers has yet been inferred.\n\n## 5. The four nonterminal-directed signs are automatic in dimension three\n\nThis is the additional sign argument specific to two physical steps.\nWrite k00=K_00 and k11=K_11, and put\n\n det2=k00*k11-lambda_01*lambda_10>0.\n\nStrict row diagonal dominance gives k00>lambda_01 and\nk11>lambda_10, so det2>0. The original value flows also give\n\n k00=lambda_01+lambda_02+2u=lambda_10+lambda_20,\n k11=lambda_10+lambda_12+2v=lambda_01+lambda_21.         (9)\n\nEvery column difference G(e_i-e_j) has last coordinate zero by(6).\nIts first two coordinates are obtained by inverting\n\n [[k00,-lambda_01],[-lambda_10,k11]].\n\nIn particular, for columns0-1 the first two coordinates are\n((k11-lambda_01)/det2,(lambda_10-k00)/det2), and for\ncolumns1-2 they are(lambda_01/det2,k00/det2).\nUsing the displayed Delta and(9) gives ALL FOUR signs\n\n F_01=-lambda_20/det2,\n F_10=-(lambda_02+2u)/det2,\n F_20=-lambda_01/det2,\n F_21=-lambda_21/det2.                                (10)\n\nTherefore the output lambda_rev is nonnegative on every pair whose\ntarget is not the terminal2. Only lambda_rev,02 and lambda_rev,12\ncould have failed signs. It is precisely a signed-terminal certificate,\nwith full PSD by(8). Apply Section3, with reversed steps(v,u), to turn\nit into an ordinary certificate. In fact Section3 automatically makes\nlambda_rev,12 positive, so only the single entry02 can require repair.\nIf it does, (4) gives the replacement explicitly.\n\nThis proves the forward implication in(A). Applying the same result\nto the reversed word proves the backward implication. This does not\nclaim that the unmodified inverse map preserves every ordinary array:\na negative incoming-terminal output can occur and is repaired here.\n\n## 6. Exact extraction at a saturated cut\n\nFirst compress zero steps as in Section2. Retain a strictly positive\nbridge1+r, positive entries in the two children, and allow either child\nto be empty. Let their capacities be A,D, with r(A+D+r)=AD,\nT=A+D+r, kappa=(T/D)^2. The prefix query set P ends at the bridge query\nL; the tail query set B begins just after it and ends at z.\n\nSuppose a full ordinary parent certificate is supplied. An affine Huber\ntrajectory with constant gradients1 has V=Q=0. A second legal scalar\nfunction has derivative clip(x,[-eta,1]), eta=r/D=A/T, and starts at A.\nThe prefix gradients are1; the bridge takes x_L=1 to-r; the tail\ngradients are-eta and end at x_z=-eta. Integration from0 defines a\nconvex differentiable1-smooth function with minimizer0. At every query\nf_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Telescoping gives V=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0.\n\nFor i in P and j in B, let R_i^a and R_j^b be their remaining child-step\nsums, so R_L^a=R_z^b=0. Direct substitution gives every cross residual:\n\n Q_ij=-(1+eta)eta R_j^b,\n Q_ji=-(1+eta)R_i^a.                                 (11)\n\nSame-block residuals are zero. Since lambda>=0 and M>=0, equality of V\nforces complementarity. Thus the only possible cross edges are i->z\nfor i in P and j->L for j in B. The two equality gradient vectors span\nthe block indicators; hence both indicators are in the full PSD kernel.\nWriting nu_i=lambda_i,z and mu_j=lambda_j,L, the row i<L of M e_B is\n-D nu_i/2, so nu_i=0. The row j<z of M e_P is\n(r mu_j-nu_L b_j)/2; its row z is(r mu_z-nu_L)/2.\nTherefore mu_j=(nu_L/r)b_j and mu_z=nu_L/r. Summing the prefix flow\nnow yields D nu_L/r-nu_L=2(A+r). Since r<D, this gives\n\n lambda_L,z=r(kappa-1),\n lambda_j,L=(kappa-1)b_j (j<z in B),\n lambda_z,L=kappa-1,                                  (12)\n\nwith all other cross entries zero. The identity\n(kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r) follows by rT=AD and verifies(12).\n\nThe accepted full join identity can now be applied as an algebraic\nidentity, without assuming that the children are primitive. Subtracting\nthe forced cross terms(12) gives the block-diagonal remainder\n\n M_parent=M_a+kappa M_b,\n M_a=V_a+sum_(i,j in P)lambda_ij Q_ij,\n M_b=V_b+sum_(i,j in B)lambda_hat_ij Q_ij,\n lambda_hat_ij=lambda_ij/kappa\n                 -(1-1/kappa)b_i*1_(i<z,j=z).         (13)\n\nEach block cancels its own function values. The formal gradient sets\nare disjoint, so each block is PSD. The prefix coefficients are ordinary.\nThe tail coefficients are nonnegative except possibly those entering z.\nOne cannot discard that possible sign defect in the supplied array.\nWhen each child has at most two positive steps, Section3 repairs the\nright child and leaves ordinary full certificates for both children.\nSection5 reverses them. The full saturated join from the accepted fact,\nwith capacities interchanged, constructs a nonnegative full certificate\nfor (rev(b),1+r,rev(a)). Section2 restores every original zero-step\ngradient. This proves(B), and hence the reversal part of(C).\n\n## 7. The saturated-middle class cannot reach the threshold\n\nA singleton full primitive certificate has step<=sqrt(2), by(1), and\ncapacity<=1+sqrt(2)<483/200. We give a short independent two-step\ncapacity bound, sufficient for the four-step corollary.\n\nFor any full primitive word of total capacity C, the legal clipped\nquadratic at a selected step h_j>1, with prefix sum P and suffix sum R,\nis obtained from derivative clip(x,[-(h_j-1)/(R+1),1]) starting at P+1.\nIts prefix gradient is1, its tail gradient is the negative threshold,\nand its terminal point equals that gradient. Since all its values\nsatisfy f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, its energy is\n\n (P+1)^2-C^2 (h_j-1)^2/(R+1)^2.\n\nNonnegativity gives C(h_j-1)_+<=(P+1)(R+1). For a two-step word, both\nendpoint steps are therefore bounded by2C/(C+1), including the case\nh_j<=1. The actual unit quadratic, started at1, also gives\n\n C |(h_0-1)(h_1-1)|<=1.                               (14)\n\nAssume a two-step certificate had C>=201/50. Set alpha=h_0-1,\nbeta=h_1-1 and rC=(C-1)/(C+1). Then\nalpha+beta=C-3 and alpha,beta<=rC. Their lower bound\nlC=C-3-rC=C-4+2/(C+1) is positive. If lC>rC the endpoint bounds are\nalready inconsistent. Otherwise alpha beta>=rC*lC, since both factors\nlie in[lC,rC] with fixed sum. The functions C,rC,lC are positive and\nstrictly increasing for C>=201/50. At that endpoint,\n\n C*rC*lC=159373101/157502500>1,\n\ncontradicting(14). Thus every two-step primitive certificate has\ncapacity strictly below201/50. This also covers its zero-step limits\nby compression or the same witnesses.\n\nAt either middle cut in a four-step word, one child has at most one\nphysical step and the other at most two. Section6 extracts ordinary\ncertificates for those children. Saturation gives\n\n T=Omega(A,D),\n Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2.\n\nThis function is increasing in both positive arguments, directly from\nthe displayed expression. Consequently\n\n T<Omega(483/200,201/50)<77/10<193/25.\n\nFor the strict penultimate inequality, at t=77/10 the increasing-root\npolynomial t^2-(483/200+201/50)t-(483/200)(201/50) equals161/5000>0,\nand t is above the nonnegative root's vertex. This proves(C).\n\n## 8. Direct-proving screen and remaining route\n\nThe named literature was checked at its exact class and performance:\n\n* Grimmer--Shu--Wang, Composing Optimized Stepsize Schedules for Gradient\n  Descent, arXiv2410.16249v2, Definition3 and Proposition2. Definition3\n  imposes eta=1/(1+sum h)=product(h_i-1), while Proposition2 proves\n  reversal of BASIC s-composable words by recursive child exchange.\n  Proposition6 translates its energy under that same rate hypothesis.\n  It does not assert reversal of our arbitrary full cone.\n* Shu--Wang, A Unified Theory of H-Duality in First-Order Methods,\n  arXiv2609.03281v1, Theorem3/Corollary3 and Section4.7 Proposition2.\n  The general statement transports unsigned certificates and retains a\n  separate coefficient-sign test. Its automatic sign theorem assumes a\n  tridiagonal REDUCED certificate, which is not a hypothesis here.\n  No external result is used as a black-box proof premise above.\n\nThe exact source HTML and readable extracted text are saved in\nlocal downloads/reversal/. Named Matlas searches were attempted and\nreturned unrelated statements; none was cited as a theorem. A PDF was\ndownloaded for2410.16249; pdftotext was unavailable, so source HTML was\nused without installing anything.\n\nTwo routes have been screened rather than silently promoted:\n\n1. The general unsigned map is already shared in4a064f93111ac5a2 and\n   fails signs on some ordinary arrays by37622e6cdcb606e6. Section5\n   resolves the sign selection ONLY in the two-step case by showing\n   that all remaining bad signs target the terminal and are repairable.\n   At four steps, negative nonterminal outputs can occur, so this\n   repair proof does not extend unchanged.\n2. A subcritical tree construction was independently recovered but\n   is already fully present in0b2dfaee171c78eb. It is not new progress\n   and remains bounded by the exact primitive DP. It cannot transport\n   threshold exclusions or supply an improving seed.\n\nA proposed below-threshold counterexample shape was also rejected\nanalytically, before claiming a certificate. Let\nh=(1+u,1+u,1+v,1+v), 0<u<1/2, 0<v<=1, C=5+2u+2v,\nand impose C*u^2*v^2=1. An actual capped quadratic with derivative\nmin(x,1), started at3+2u, has its first two gradients1 and then tail\nquadratic gradients1,-v,v^2. All sampled x_i<=1 after the first pair,\nso these are legal for this fixed convex1-smooth function, with\nminimizer0. Every sample satisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 and the final\npoint equals its gradient. Its exact energy is\n\n (3+2u)^2-C^2 v^4=(3+2u)^2-u^(-4)<0,\n\nbecause (3+2u)u^2<1 for0<u<1/2. Thus this entire family cannot be the\nfeasible side of a reversal counterexample. This is a failed candidate\nfamily, not a counterexample to reversal itself.\n\nThe outstanding problem remains an ordinary full certificate for an\narbitrary four-step word versus rev(h). In particular, neither the\nremaining strict-cut threshold region nor a four-step counterexample\nhas been decided. The next round must address genuinely nonterminal\ninverse signs or construct an actual asymmetric feasible word; it must\nnot repeat the basic/subcritical theorem or bad-supplied-array examples.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["8211d6128deabdf3", "bdead8bb6b6d55a7", "4a064f93111ac5a2", "f3d96c901566e96b", "f7cee3034df8cb7f"], "artifact": "research/full_primitive_reversal_short_blocks.md"}, "glossary": {"rev(h)": "The physical word h in reverse chronological order.", "signed-terminal certificate": "A formal identity V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij with exact value cancellation and full PSD remainder, permitting negative multipliers only when their target is the terminal query.", "K_rev": "Grounded matrix for the reversed signed certificate constructed in Sections4--5.", "det2": "The positive determinant K_00 K_11-lambda_01 lambda_10 of the leading2x2 grounded matrix for a two-positive-step word.", "F_rev": "The inverse-difference matrix Delta K^(-1)Delta, denoted F locally in the proof.", "Delta_rev": "The symmetric3x3 matrix [[0,-1,1],[-1,1,0],[1,0,0]], denoted Delta locally in the proof."}}
{"id": "eff9ce341637cc98", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T10:00:19.442583+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C92 complete UNVERIFIED joint draft: exact dense signed physical-tail construction and an all-amplitude/all-value/all-anchor/free-terminal ceiling for the entire one-initial-sign constant-correlation class. On original H_m, the supremal original-B payoff tends exactly to Z_1/[2e(1+sqrt2)^2]<1/5, uniformly over every horizon-dependent correlation tau in(0,1). Explicit positive character laws attain this limit; a separate fixed recipe gives B_n V>1/150 for every m>=4 with least atom1/[8n(n+1)]. An associative legal tail rule retains all signed history and true radius1. This is a scoped witness-family result, not an upper bound on unrestricted W or an answer to the target.", "evidence": "# C92: an exact signed tail rule and its complete asymptotic class limit\n\nStatus: **UNVERIFIED joint proof draft**. This is actual runtime launch\nround 2, cumulative round 23 after baseline 21, under the unchanged cap 23.\nThe previous runtime round contained both C90 and C91; its internal-stage\nround count was corrected in finding `92d17fda8c9a843f`. No numerical\nevaluation, scan, replay, solver, installation, subordinate agent, or\nverifier call is used here.\n\nAccepted mathematical predecessors are `1252b8823bb5a290` (all-pair smooth\nconvex interpolation and its explicit conjugate), `0e7e78056dc6258c`\n(the scalar moment elimination), `b98bb807d514e80d` (the original benchmark\nand exact single-cut example), and `04219554e41d9750` (the original dyadic\nbenchmark limit). No unverified C90 or C91 assertion is a proof premise.\nAll new assertions used below are proved together in this document.\n\nThe original arbitrary-schedule problem remains open. The result below\nsettles the strength of one dense signed geometry, not the original problem.\n\n## 1. Original schedule, class, and claims\n\nFor each integer m>=4 set\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,\n    c=sigma(1+1/m), a=delta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nUse exactly h=H_m, so h_t=1+a*c^(nu_2(t+1)) for 0<=t<n.\nAll occurrences of a and c retain the enclosing m. Put\n\n    ell=1+a=h_0=h_(n-1), S=sum_(t<n)h_t,\n    T=sum_(t=1)^(n-1)h_t=S-ell,\n    K_first_m=a^2/(1+2T).\n\nHere K_first_m is the actual first-cut scalar payoff from accepted\n`b98bb807d514e80d`. It is not a new benchmark. B_n is the ORIGINAL\nZhang--Jiang denominator, so the benchmark objective is 1/B_n.\nThe elementary bounds 6/25<a<1/2 give 1<ell<3/2.\n\nDefine the following restricted class of legal instances on this schedule.\nNormalize smoothness to 1, choose a closest minimizer as 0, and normalize\nthe true initial distance to ||X_0||=1. Write g_i=grad F(X_i),\nf_i=F(X_i)-F(0), with the actual GD displacement\n\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i*g_i.\n\nIf a queried gradient is zero, the terminal gap is zero. Otherwise write\ng_i=A_i*u_i, A_i>0, ||u_i||=1, for 0<=i<n. The sole queried-Gram\nrestriction is that there is a number tau in (0,1) with\n\n    <u_0,u_i>=-tau                  (1<=i<n),\n    <u_i,u_j>=tau                   (1<=i<j<n).          (1)\n\nEvery amplitude, initial anchor, sampled value, and actual function is\notherwise free. Tau may depend arbitrarily on m. In particular the\nunqueried terminal gradient g_n is unrestricted. The Gram in (1) is\n(1-tau)I+tau*eta*eta^T, where eta_0=1 and eta_i=-1 for i>=1.\nIt has no orthogonal queried pair and has a negative initial correlation\nwith every later query. Thus C89's positive-history premise fails and its\nlarge orthogonal selection is absent.\n\nLet V_best_m be the supremum of 2f_n over this class, including all dimensions.\nThe proposed joint conclusions are:\n\n1. A fully explicit dense character construction, all original pair\n   constraints, one static function, unique minimizer, and radius 1 are\n   given below. It has an associative physical-tail boundary rule with\n   all history and value costs retained.\n2. For every m>=4, one specified construction has B_n*2f_n>1/150 and\n   minimum character atom exactly 1/[8n(n+1)]. No scalar strictification\n   or unpriced anchor component is required.\n3. More strongly, the ENTIRE class (1) has the exact asymptotic strength\n\n       lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)*V_best_m\n          = Z_1/[2e*sigma^2] < 1/5,                    (2)\n\n   where Z_1=lim sigma^(-m)B_(2^m-1) is the accepted ray constant and\n   e=lim (1+1/m)^m. This includes arbitrary horizon-dependent tau and\n   free terminal gradients, not merely the displayed construction.\n\nThe upper bound in (2) is about this family of lower witnesses. It is\nNOT an upper bound on unrestricted W_n(h).\n\n## 2. A direct construction on an arbitrary eligible physical word\n\nFirst let n>=2, h_0=1+a with a>0, and choose 0<tau<1 such that\n\n    h_i>=1/tau, 1<=i<n.                                (3)\n\nChoose unit vectors u_0,...,u_n with exactly the signed Gram (1), now\nincluding i=n with negative sign. Such vectors are realized explicitly\nin Section 6. Choose q>0 and define\n\n    A_0=1, A_i=q*tau^(i-1) for 1<=i<=n,\n    g_i=A_i*u_i, X_0=g_0,\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nDefine the actual tail moments\n\n    P_i=sum_(t=1)^(i-1)h_t*A_t,\n    E_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1)h_t*A_t^2,  1<=i<=n.\n\nEmpty sums are zero. Let b>0 satisfy\n\n    A_n+b/A_n+tau*P_n <= tau*a.                        (4)\n\nSet f_0=1/2 and\n\n    f_i=A_i^2/2+b+tau^2*E_i, 1<=i<=n,                 (5)\n\nand include the optimum jet (0,0,0). These are values on original indices;\nno trajectory is compressed or reset. The following sections verify EVERY\nordered interpolation residual\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.         (6)\n\nThe necessity and sufficiency of Q_ij<=0, with the explicit construction\nof one function, are accepted in `1252b8823bb5a290`.\n\n## 3. Every finite tail pair, with the original displacement\n\nFor 1<=i<j<=n put\n\n    W_ij=sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t*A_t,\n    D_ij=f_i-f_j=(A_i^2-A_j^2)/2\n                           +tau^2*sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t*A_t^2.\n\nThe identity for D_ij follows from (5), not from a compressed update.\nThe actual signed inner products give the following exact allowed interval:\n\n    L_ij <= D_ij <= U_ij,\n    L_ij=tau*A_j*W_ij+(A_i^2+A_j^2-2tau*A_i*A_j)/2,\n    U_ij=h_i*A_i^2+tau*A_i*sum_(t=i+1)^(j-1)h_t*A_t\n                         -(A_i^2+A_j^2-2tau*A_i*A_j)/2. (7)\n\nHere Q_ji=L_ij-D_ij and Q_ij=D_ij-U_ij. In particular every skipped\nhistory term h_t*<g_i,g_t> is present in (7).\n\nThe lower inequality follows from the identity\n\n    D_ij-L_ij\n      =sum_(t=i)^(j-1)tau*h_t*A_t*(tau*A_t-A_j)\n                          +A_j*(tau*A_i-A_j) >=0.      (8)\n\nFor each summand t<=j-1, tau*A_t>=A_j, because A_(t+1)=tau*A_t.\nThe last term is also nonnegative. For j=i+1 both terms vanish, giving\nthe exact reverse-row equality Q_(i+1,i)=0.\n\nFor the upper inequality put d=j-i. Direct expansion gives\n\n    (U_ij-D_ij)/A_i^2\n      =(h_i-1)(1-tau^2)\n       +sum_(k=1)^(d-1)(h_(i+k)-1)*tau^(k+1)*(1-tau^(k+1))\n       +J_d,\n\n    J_d=-tau^2+tau^(d+1)\n                   +sum_(k=1)^(d-1)(tau^(k+1)-tau^(2k+2)). (9)\n\nOne has J_1=0 and\n\n    J_(d+1)-J_d=tau^(d+2)*(1-tau^d)>=0.\n\nAll coefficients in the first two terms of (9) are nonnegative because\nh_i>=1. Thus U_ij-D_ij>=0. This proves both orientations for every\ntail pair, not just adjacent samples.\n\n## 4. Every optimum and initial pair; the exact scalar boundary\n\nThe negative initial history is retained exactly:\n\n    <g_i,X_i>=tau*A_i*(a-P_i), 1<=i<=n.               (10)\n\nThe contribution of the first displacement is +h_0*tau*A_i, while\n<g_i,X_0>=-tau*A_i. Deleting that signed initial term would change (10).\n\nThe optimum residual Q_(i,star) is nonpositive precisely when\n\n    R_i:=A_i+b/A_i+tau*P_i+tau^2*E_i/A_i <= tau*a.\n\nFor 1<=i<n, expansion using A_(i+1)=tau*A_i gives\n\n    R_(i+1)-R_i\n      =(1-tau)[(tau*h_i-1)A_i\n                    +tau*E_(i+1)/A_i+b/(tau*A_i)] >=0. (11)\n\nCondition (3) is used here. Consequently every optimum upper row follows\nfrom the SINGLE final boundary (4). Conversely the final row requires\n(4), so this is an exact elimination for this chosen construction.\nThe other optimum orientation is\n\n    Q_(star,i)=-b-tau^2*E_i<0.\n\nAt i=0, X_0=g_0 and f_0=1/2 give both optimum residuals zero.\n\nFor i>=1, direct calculation gives\n\n    Q_(i,0)=Q_(i,star),\n    Q_(0,i)=-a-b-tau^2*E_i+tau*(P_i+A_i).             (12)\n\nIf i<n, then P_n-P_i>=h_i*A_i>=A_i. Hence\ntau*(P_i+A_i)<=tau*P_n<=tau*a by (4).\nIf i=n, (4) gives tau*P_n+A_n<=tau*a, so\ntau*(P_n+A_n)<=tau*a-(1-tau)A_n<=tau*a.\nIn either case Q_(0,i)<=-(1-tau)a-b-tau^2*E_i<0.\n\nEquations (7)--(12), together with the two zero rows at i=0, check ALL\noriginal directed pairs including the terminal. For the smallest physical\nblock, indices 0,1 and star, they give Q_(1,0)=Q_(1,star)<=0 and\nQ_(0,1)<0 with <g_0,g_1>=-tau*q<0. This block is part of the original\nword with its actual h_0, and already has genuinely signed history.\n\n## 5. One static function, values, and exact closest distance\n\nPut y_i=X_i-g_i and d_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Then y_0=0,d_0=0 and\nd_i=b+tau^2*E_i>0 for i>=1. Define on the same Euclidean space\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,max_(1<=i<=n)(<y_i,v>-d_i)},\n    F(X)=sup_v(<X,v>-H(v)).                            (13)\n\nBy the explicit all-pair interpolation construction in accepted\n`1252b8823bb5a290`, F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and\n1-smooth, and F(X_i)=f_i, grad F(X_i)=g_i for every i, including 0 and n.\nThus its actual GD is exactly the prescribed original trajectory.\n\nAll d_i with i>=1 are strictly positive, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 in a\nneighborhood of zero. Also H(v)>=||v||^2/2 everywhere. It follows that\nF(0)=0. For any X!=0, take a sufficiently small t in (0,1) with tX in\nthat neighborhood. Then\n\n    F(X)>=(t-t^2/2)||X||^2>0.\n\nTherefore argmin F={0}. Its true initial squared distance is exactly\n||X_0||^2=||g_0||^2=1. No inverse-Gram or orthogonal-anchor radius cost is\nhidden. Multiplication of F by L and use of physical steps h_i/L give\nthe same normalized payoff for arbitrary L>0.\n\n## 6. A positive finite character law\n\nLet d be any integer with 2^d>=n+1. In the (d+1)-bit label space use\n\n    lambda_0=(0,binary(0)),\n    lambda_i=(1,binary(i)), 1<=i<=n.\n\nThese labels do not permute the physical indices. Let s range over this\nbinary space, let e_sig have only its first bit equal to 1, and put\n\n    D=2^(d+1),\n    w_s=tau*1_(s=e_sig)+(1-tau)/D,\n    (u_i)_s=sqrt(w_s)*(-1)^(s dot lambda_i).            (14)\n\nAll weights are positive and sum to one. Summing the binary characters\nby pairing a differing bit gives\n\n    <u_i,u_j>=tau*eta_i*eta_j+(1-tau)*1_(i=j).\n\nThis is the required full signed Gram, not a PSD-only existence claim:\npositions, all values, all original residuals and F were supplied above.\nThe explicit anchor is simply X_0=u_0. On H_m one may take d=m,D=2N.\nThe least atom is (1-tau)/(2N).\n\n## 7. Exact payoff and an associative physical block rule\n\nPut\n\n    r=tau^(n-1), H_tail=sum_(t=1)^(n-1)h_t*tau^(t-1).\n\nThen A_n=q*r and P_n=q*H_tail. For fixed q, the largest permitted b in\n(4) is\n\n    b=q*r*(tau*a-q*(tau*H_tail+r)).                   (15)\n\nWhen b>0, (13) has the unique minimizer just proved. Its normalized\nterminal payoff is\n\n    V=2f_n=2q*r*tau*a-q^2*r*(2tau*H_tail+r).\n\nCompleting the square in q gives the maximum, at\n\n    q=tau*a/(2tau*H_tail+r),\n    b=q^2*r*tau*H_tail>0,\n    V_tau=tau^2*a^2*r/(2tau*H_tail+r).                 (16)\n\nThis is an algebraic formula, not a numerical parameter evaluation.\n\nFor a consecutive tail block p=(p_0,...,p_(k-1)), define\n\n    alpha_p=tau^k,\n    H_p=sum_(j=0)^(k-1)p_j*tau^j,\n    J_p=sum_(j=0)^(k-1)p_j*tau^(2j).\n\nIf it starts at tail amplitude A, cumulative moment P, and value f, its\nactual end state is\n\n    A_out=alpha_p*A,\n    P_out=P+A*H_p,\n    f_out=f-A^2*(1-alpha_p^2)/2-tau^2*A^2*J_p.         (17)\n\nFor the physical concatenation p followed by qword,\n\n    alpha_(p qword)=alpha_p*alpha_qword,\n    H_(p qword)=H_p+alpha_p*H_qword,\n    J_(p qword)=J_p+alpha_p^2*J_qword.                 (18)\n\nThese identities follow by splitting the defining sums at the actual\nindex k. They prove associativity without resetting P, A, or the enclosing\nschedule parameters. All cross-block Q rows are already covered by\n(7)--(12). The final boundary (4) prices the complete history. This is a\nlegal iterable rule for this restricted tail geometry, not an improving\nprimitive-capacity composition theorem.\n\nSince tau*H_tail>=r*T, (16) also gives\n\n    V_tau<=tau^2*a^2/(2T+1)<K_first_m.                       (19)\n\nThus even before the wider obstruction below, the exact block rule does\nnot improve the scalar first-cut lower bound.\n\n## 8. Quantitative calibration on every original H_m\n\nChoose tau=1-1/(4n). For n>=15,\n1/tau<=60/59<31/25<ell, so (3) holds at every original tail step.\nThe character law's minimum atom is exactly 1/[8nN]. Since H_tail<=T\nand r<=1, (16) and Bernoulli's inequality yield\n\n    V_tau >= tau^(n+1)*K_first_m\n           >=[1-(n+1)/(4n)]*K_first_m >=(11/15)*K_first_m.        (20)\n\nFor completeness the original benchmark comparison uses its exact mass\nrecurrences, not the physical mass as a substitute. The accepted joins are\n\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l).\n\nBoth joins are increasing and G>=Omega: after squaring the nonnegative\nquantities 3u+sqrt(v^2+8uv) and sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2), their squared difference\nis 8u^2+2uv+6u*sqrt(v^2+8uv)>=0. Induction gives B_n>=C_n, and the\nbalanced primitive split gives B_(2^m-1)>=sigma^m.\n\nCounting 2^(m-k-1) occurrences of level k in the actual word gives\n\n    S=N-1+r_sum*(c^m-N),\n    r_sum=delta/[c(c-2)], 0<r_sum<1.                  (21)\n\nHere c>sigma>2, and c(c-2)>sigma(sigma-2)=1>delta. Hence\nS<c^m-1 and 2T+1<2c^m<6sigma^m. The last inequality uses\n(1+1/m)^m<3, obtained by the binomial expansion and sum 1/k!<3.\nFinally a=(sqrt(2)-1)(m-1)/(m+1)>6/25 for m>=4. Therefore\n\n    B_n*V_tau >(11/15)*a^2/6\n              >396/56250 >1/150.                    (22)\n\nThis proves a fully realized, quantitatively priced signed construction\non every original enclosing horizon, while (19) records its limitation.\n\n## 9. Arbitrary amplitudes: an original tail-pair restriction\n\nWe now return to the ENTIRE class (1), with arbitrary amplitudes, values,\nanchor, and free terminal. Set k=n-1, so the last queried sample is k.\nNormalize using the true closest-minimizer distance as in Section 1.\n\nFor 1<=i<j<=k, add Q_ij and Q_ji. The accepted necessary inequality is\n\n    <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j> >= ||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nIts LEFT side is exactly\n\n    h_i*A_i*(A_i-tau*A_j)\n      +tau*(A_i-A_j)*sum_(t=i+1)^(j-1)h_t*A_t.        (23)\n\nIf A_j>=A_i/tau, both terms in (23) are nonpositive. The right side is\nA_i^2+A_j^2-2tau*A_i*A_j>0 because 0<tau<1 and both amplitudes are\npositive. This contradiction proves\n\n    A_i>tau*A_j, 1<=i<j<=k.                           (24)\n\nNo nonnegative-history simplification was made across the initial sample.\nThe assertion is only within the tail where its pair correlations are\nactually positive.\n\n## 10. The true anchor and a free terminal: a sharp limiting upper bound\n\nWrite A_0=||g_0||. Optimum cocoercivity gives\n<X_0,g_0>>=A_0^2, and A_0<=||X_0||=1. Set z=<X_0,u_0>, so z>=A_0.\nResolve the unit vector u_k=-tau*u_0+sqrt(1-tau^2)*v with v perpendicular\nto u_0 and ||v||=1. Then\n\n    <X_0,u_k> <= -tau*z+sqrt(1-tau^2)*sqrt(1-z^2).\n\nThe right side is decreasing for 0<=z<=1, so it is at most its value at\nz=A_0. Put T_pre=sum_(t=1)^(k-1)h_t=T-ell. The original optimum row at k,\nincluding the negative contribution of g_0 and every positive tail term,\ntherefore gives, using (24),\n\n    f_k <= A_k[tau*a*A_0+sqrt(1-tau^2)*sqrt(1-A_0^2)]\n                               -(tau^2*T_pre+1/2)A_k^2.\n\nCauchy--Schwarz bounds the bracket by\n\n    M_tau=sqrt(tau^2*a^2+1-tau^2).\n\nThe actual final step has length ell<2. Smoothness at X_k gives\n\n    f_n<=f_k-ell*(1-ell/2)*A_k^2.\n\nMaximizing the resulting quadratic in A_k proves the uniform class bound\n\n    2f_n <= U_angle(tau)\n      :=[tau^2*a^2+1-tau^2]\n                    /[1+2tau^2*T_pre+ell*(2-ell)].    (25)\n\nThis does not impose any law on g_n. Its initial norm was the TRUE radius,\nand neither a translated reset nor an unrecorded anchor adjustment occurs.\nRelative to the first-cut payoff, (25) implies\n\n    U_angle(tau)/K_first_m\n      <=[1+(tau^(-2)-1)/a^2]\n                     *(1+2T)/(1+2T-ell^2).           (26)\n\nIn particular, as tau tends to 1 this ratio tends to 1 uniformly over m\nbecause a>6/25, ell<3/2 and T>=n-1.\n\n## 11. Small correlations: a priced moment bound on the actual tail\n\nThere are M=n-1 tail queries 1,...,k. For consecutive tail samples, the\noriginal reverse interpolation inequality reads\n\n    f_i-f_(i+1) >= (A_i^2+A_(i+1)^2)/2\n                          +tau*(h_i-1)*A_i*A_(i+1)\n                    >= (A_i^2+A_(i+1)^2)/2.           (27)\n\nAt each tail sample, the optimum row and the actual displacement from X_1\ngive\n\n    <X_1,g_i> >= f_i+A_i^2/2.\n\nAll added terms in this last relation are tau*h_t*A_i*A_t>=0 for\n1<=t<i. Write b_tail=f_k-A_k^2/2>=0. Summing (27) gives\n\n    <X_1,u_i> >= [b_tail+sum_(j=i)^k A_j^2]/A_i >0.   (28)\n\nFor real coefficients t_i, (1) gives the elementary Gram estimate\n\n    ||sum_(i=1)^k t_i*u_i||^2\n       =(1-tau)sum_i t_i^2+tau*(sum_i t_i)^2\n       <=[1+(M-1)tau]*sum_i t_i^2.\n\nApply Cauchy--Schwarz with t_i=<X_1,u_i>. It follows that\n\n    sum_i <X_1,u_i>^2 <=[1+(M-1)tau]||X_1||^2.        (29)\n\nThe original first step costs no hidden radius: since ell<2 and\n<X_0,g_0>>=A_0^2,\n\n    ||X_1||^2<=1-ell*(2-ell)*A_0^2<=1.\n\nThe scalar elimination proved in accepted `0e7e78056dc6258c`, proof\nSection 5, applies to (28)--(29): if q_i=A_i^2 and T_i=b_tail+sum_(j>=i)q_j,\nit bounds 2f_k by beta_(M-1) times the upper budget for sum T_i^2/q_i.\nHere beta_j=4/[1+sqrt(1+2c_j)]^2, c_0=0,\nc_(j+1)=c_j+2+2sqrt(c_j+1), with beta_j<=2/j^2 for j>=1.\nThe actual last step cannot increase f. Hence\n\n    2f_n <= [1+(n-2)tau]*beta_(n-2)\n           <= 2[1+(n-2)tau]/(n-2)^2.                 (30)\n\nThis is an elementary extension with its full Gram cost written out.\nNo orthogonality of these actual tail gradients is asserted.\n\n## 12. Intermediate correlations: exact adjacent ratios\n\nFor two consecutive tail queries the same cocoercivity inequality gives\n\n    (h_i-1)A_i^2-A_(i+1)^2\n                    +(2-h_i)tau*A_i*A_(i+1)>=0.\n\nFor z>0 define the positive root\n\n    r_tau(z)=[-tau*(z-1)+sqrt(tau^2*(z-1)^2+4z)]/2.\n\nThen A_(i+1)/A_i<=r_tau(h_i-1). The root obeys r_tau(z)>tau and is\nstrictly increasing in z. A convenient exact formula, with natural log,\nis\n\n    phi_tau(x):=log r_tau(exp x)\n       =x/2-asinh(tau*sinh(x/2)).                     (31)\n\nIndeed divide r^2+tau(z-1)r-z=0 by r*sqrt(z) and solve the resulting\nhyperbolic-sine identity. Thus phi is odd, phi(0)=0, and\n\n    phi'(x)=1/2-tau*cosh(x/2)\n                              /[2sqrt(1+tau^2*sinh^2(x/2))]>0,\n    phi''(x)=-tau*(1-tau^2)*sinh(x/2)\n                              /[4(1+tau^2*sinh^2(x/2))^(3/2)]. (32)\n\nThe derivative positivity follows by squaring: the denominator under its\nsquare root exceeds tau^2*cosh^2(x/2) by 1-tau^2. On x>0, phi is\nstrictly concave. These formulas also prove r_tau(z)>tau by its limit\nas z decreases to zero and strict monotonicity.\n\nThe first queried norm is bounded without assuming it shares a positive\ncorrelation. Write d=g_1-g_0. Cocoercivity and X_1-X_0=-ell*g_0 give\n-ell*<g_0,d>>=||d||^2, whence\n\n    ||g_1||^2<=||g_0||^2+(1-2/ell)||d||^2<=||g_0||^2<=1.\n\nDefine the product over ALL original steps\n\n    P_all=product_(t=0)^(n-1) r_tau(h_t-1).\n\nThe first and last physical steps both have excess a. Multiplying only\nthe legitimate tail inequalities i=1,...,n-2 therefore gives\n\n    A_k <= P_all/r_tau(a)^2 <= tau^(-2)*P_all.         (33)\n\nThis product does not assume any condition on the free terminal gradient.\n\n## 13. Ruler counts create a uniform strict product loss\n\nPut Lc=log c. Since the physical excess at level j is delta*c^(j-1)\nwith delta<1, monotonicity in (31) and the original counts give\n\n    log P_all\n      <=(N/2)[-phi(Lc)+sum_(j=2)^(m-1)2^(-j)*phi((j-1)Lc)].\n\nConcavity and phi(0)=0 imply phi(r*Lc)<=r*phi(Lc) for integer r>=1.\nKeep the strict deficit at j=3 separately and write\n\n    Delta_tau(c)=2phi(Lc)-phi(2Lc)>0.\n\nThe finite identity sum_(j=2)^(m-1)(j-1)/2^j=1-m/2^(m-1) yields\n\n    log P_all <= -m*phi(Lc)-(N/16)*Delta_tau(c).        (34)\n\nThis retains all original levels and their actual multiplicities.\n\nFor an explicit bound, c>=sigma>2 and c<4 for m>=4. Hence\nLc>1/2 and cosh(Lc)<5/2. The identity\n\n    Delta_tau(c)=integral_(t=0)^Lc integral_(s=t)^(t+Lc)\n                                           [-phi''(s)] ds dt\n\ncontains the rectangle Lc/2<=t<=Lc, Lc<=s<=3Lc/2. On this rectangle,\nsinh(s/2)>1/4, and\n(1+tau^2*sinh^2(s/2))^(3/2)<=cosh(Lc)^3<125/8.\nIts area is Lc^2/4>1/16. Substitution into (32) proves\n\n    Delta_tau(c)>=tau*(1-tau^2)/4000\n                         >=tau*(1-tau)/4000.          (35)\n\nFor clarity Lc>1/2 follows from log 2=integral_1^2 dt/t>1/2;\nthe other bounds are direct inequalities, not numerical evaluation.\nCombining (33)--(35), and dropping the nonpositive -m*phi(Lc), gives\n\n    A_k <=tau^(-2)*exp[-N*tau*(1-tau)/64000].\n\nFinally retain the signed original displacement in the optimum row:\n\n    f_k<=<g_k,X_k>\n       =<g_k,X_0>+ell*tau*A_0*A_k\n                    -tau*A_k*sum_(t=1)^(k-1)h_t*A_t\n       <=(1+ell*tau)*A_k <=(5/2)*A_k.\n\nThe final physical step is again nonincreasing for f. Thus the entire\nclass with free terminal satisfies\n\n    2f_n <=5tau^(-2)*exp[-N*tau*(1-tau)/64000].         (36)\n\nThis closes fixed correlations bounded away from 0 and 1 exponentially.\nIt does not discard the regime tau tending to either endpoint.\n\n## 14.
