There is a genuine positive joint value price.\nThat price adds no negative primitive-energy test beyond the COMPLETE\nvalue-optimized scalar channel. A full original certificate is also\ngiven for one below-threshold member, to prove the domain is not\nalready excluded by the older necessary conditions.\n\nThis does not give a threshold seed, prove a global no-seed theorem, or\ndecide the original exact-profile Goal. The goal's convention stays\nW_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), hence W=2R.\n\nNotation: all new local letters a,b,c,d,alpha,beta,u,v,p_i,z_i,ell,m,\nt,U_alpha,U_beta,e_i^theta,d_i^theta,r_i^theta,J_theta,X_i,Y_i,\nE_tail,E_mix,j_min,Conj,A_i,MinHull,R_true,kappa,delta in this draft\nmean their versions with suffix29. They do not redefine project\nsymbols with the same printed names. The schedule h, mass s, original\nQ_ij, Lambda_ij, L_flow, T, D_prim and M_prim retain their stated roles.\n\n## 1. Actual word, curvatures, amplitudes, and all signs\n\nFix a word in the domain\n\n    h=(a,b,c,d),\n    7/5<=a<=7/4,   2<b<=9/4,   0<=c,d<=a.                 (1)\n\nSet s=a+b+c+d and C=1+s. These are variable, not a fixed-capacity\nslice. Define\n\n    beta_max=min({1} union {1/c:c>0} union {1/d:d>0}),\n    1/b<=alpha<=1/a,      1/a<=beta<=beta_max,\n    u,v>=0.                                               (2)\n\nThe beta interval is nonempty because c,d<=a and a>1. Negative initial\namplitudes give equivalent geometries by coordinate reflection, so\nnonnegative u,v loses nothing. Both pure-amplitude endpoints are retained.\n\nFor i=0,...,4 define\n\n    p_i=product_(0<=j<i)(1-alpha*h_j),\n    z_i=product_(0<=j<i)(1-beta*h_j),\n    x_i=(u*p_i,v*z_i),\n    g_i=(alpha*u*p_i,beta*v*z_i).                          (3)\n\nThe empty products are1. These are the exact samples of ONE static,\nconvex, 1-smooth base function\n\n    F_base(x,y)=alpha*x^2/2+beta*y^2/2.\n\nThe gradient-descent recursion is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. All original\nindices, including the terminal index4, are present.\n\nPut ell=1-a*alpha and m=ell*(b*alpha-1). Then\n\n    p=(1,ell,-m,-m*(1-c*alpha),\n                         -m*(1-c*alpha)*(1-d*alpha)).      (4)\n\nThus ell,m>=0. Both tail factors lie in[0,1]. The fast samples obey\n\n    z_1<=0,  z_2=(a*beta-1)*(b*beta-1)>=0,\n    0<=z_4<=z_3<=z_2<=15/16<1.                            (5)\n\nFor the last bound, (a*beta-1)*(b*beta-1) increases for beta>=1/a:\nits derivative is2ab*beta-a-b>=b-a>0. Hence it is at most\n(a-1)*(b-1)<=(3/4)*(5/4)=15/16. The two beta-tail factors are\nnonnegative by(2).\n\nOn the open domain with strict curvature endpoints, 0<c,d<1/beta,\nand u,v>0, the slow signs are +,+,-,-,- and the fast signs are\n+,-,+,+,+. Both modes survive to index4; neither is an early-killed\nmode. This open set is nonempty even for the full-certificate word in\nSection9. Curvature endpoints, c=0, d=0, and all product-zero cases\nare included in(1)--(5). The limiting face b=2 obeys the same formulas,\nalthough the selected family itself has b>2.\n\n## 2. The original primitive interface and exact terminal-source price\n\nFor distinct i,j in{0,1,2,3,4}, use\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nAll TWENTY ordered constraints Q_ij<=0 are imposed. The primitive\nenergy is exactly\n\n    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)\n             +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_4||^2.   (6)\n\nFor the original full certificate retain all twenty Lambda_ij>=0,\nzero diagonal, and define\n\n    e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T,  L_flow=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2a,-2b,-2c,-2d,2s)^T,\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n    D_prim=diag(a(2-a),b(2-b),c(2-c),d(2-d),-s(s+2)).\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 gives the exact identity\n\n    L_flow^T e=omega,\n    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow*T+T^T*L_flow^T)/2,\n    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                          =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>.    (7)\n\nA seed still requires M_prim positive semidefinite on ALL of R^5.\nNo coordinate, sign, pair, or zero-step formal gradient is deleted.\n\nWrite U_theta=theta*(1-theta)/2 for theta=alpha,beta. Substitution in\nQ_ij at the base values gives the symmetric nonnegative slack\n\n    w_ij=-Q_ij(F_base)\n         =u^2 U_alpha(p_i-p_j)^2+v^2 U_beta(z_i-z_j)^2.    (8)\n\nFor arbitrary new values f_i=F_base(x_i)+epsilon_i the complete\nconstraint system is epsilon_i-epsilon_j<=w_ij. Let D_i be the\nshortest w-cost from the ORIGINAL terminal source4 to node i in the\ncomplete five-node graph, with D_4=0. A shortest simple path exists:\nweights are nonnegative and cycles can be removed, including zero-cost\ncycles. Following a path from4 to i gives epsilon_i-epsilon_4>=-D_i.\nAll h_i are nonnegative, so the simultaneous all-value minimum is\n\n    V_min=V_base-2sum_(i<4)h_i D_i.                       (9)\n\nIt is attained by epsilon_i=-D_i. Indeed the distance triangle\ninequality D_j<=D_i+w_ij proves every ordered residual is nonpositive.\n\nThe matching auxiliary flow sends2h_i units from4 to each i<4 along\nan attaining path. Conversely w_ij>=D_j-D_i and(7)'s flow give\nsum Lambda_ij w_ij>=2sum h_i D_i. This proves the exact price and its\nterminal-source orientation. An optimal auxiliary price flow is not\nasserted to be a full PSD certificate.\n\n## 3. Scalar distances and energies: explicit formulas\n\nFor real points t_i on a line, the shortest path with squared-distance\nedge weights from one sampled value to another consists of all sampled\nvalues between them in monotone order, with ties allowed. Here is a\nproof that also covers endpoints and equal values. List the distinct\nordered sampled values and their nonnegative adjacent gaps. The square\nof any edge spanning several gaps is at least the sum of the squared\ngaps it spans. Every path between the selected endpoints crosses every\ngap between them at least once. Its cost is therefore at least the\nsum of those squared gaps; the monotone path attains that sum. Excursions\noutside the interval cannot improve it. Tied nodes add zero gaps and\nremain valid original nodes.\n\nFor q_i=p_i or z_i define\n\n    e_1(q)=(q_1-q_4)^2,\n    e_2(q)=(q_2-q_3)^2+(q_3-q_4)^2,\n    e_3(q)=(q_3-q_4)^2,\n    e_4(q)=0.                                           (10)\n\nFor the slow coordinate, the complete order is\n\n    p_2<=p_3<=p_4<=p_1<=p_0=1.\n\nFor the fast coordinate, it is\n\n    z_1<=z_4<=z_3<=z_2<z_0=1.\n\nConsequently the unweighted scalar distances from terminal4 are\n\n    d_0^alpha=(1-p_1)^2+(p_1-p_4)^2,\n    d_0^beta =(1-z_2)^2+(z_2-z_3)^2+(z_3-z_4)^2,\n    d_i^alpha=e_i(p), d_i^beta=e_i(z),  i=1,2,3,4.         (11)\n\nDefine the base scalar energy for any q_i=product_(j<i)(1-theta*h_j)\nby direct substitution into(6):\n\n    B_theta=theta sum_(i<4)h_i[1+theta(1-h_i)]q_i^2\n                      -s*theta[1+C*theta]q_4^2.          (12)\n\nThe exact all-value scalar minimum at that curvature and unit initial\namplitude is\n\n    J_theta=B_theta-theta(1-theta)sum_(i<4)h_i d_i^theta,\n                         theta=alpha or beta.            (13)\n\nEquations(9)--(11), or the one-dimensional conjugate in Section8,\nprove that this is attained by an actual scalar smooth convex function\nwith a minimizer. It is not just the energy of the original quadratic:\nALL scalar values have been optimized using ALL original pairs.\n\n## 4. Exact joint distances and the four competing prices\n\nFor the four nodes1,4,3,2, both coordinates have the same monotone\nnode order, with opposite coordinate directions. The path along this\nchain between any pair simultaneously attains both scalar shortest\ndistances. Node0 is outside the coordinate range of this chain in each\ncoordinate, so it cannot lower either scalar distance. Thus the joint\ndistances to i=1,2,3 are exactly\n\n    D_i=u^2 U_alpha e_i(p)+v^2 U_beta e_i(z).              (14)\n\nA shortest simple path to0 has a last chain node k in{1,4,3,2}.\nIts prefix can be replaced by a shortest chain path, so exactly\n\n    D_0=min_(k in{1,4,3,2})\n       [u^2 U_alpha r_k(p)+v^2 U_beta r_k(z)],\n    r_k(q)=e_k(q)+(1-q_k)^2.                             (15)\n\nThis argument examines every possible path; it does not assume that a\nchronological path must remain shortest after a sign change.\n\nDefine the ENERGY price increments\n\n    X_k=2a U_alpha[r_k(p)-d_0^alpha],\n    Y_k=2a U_beta [r_k(z)-d_0^beta].                      (16)\n\nThey already include the original sink0 demand2a. All are nonnegative,\nX_1=0, Y_2=0. Expanding differences of squares gives\n\n    X_4=4a U_alpha(1-p_1)(p_1-p_4),\n    X_3=X_4+4a U_alpha(p_4-p_3)(1-p_3),\n    X_2=X_3+4a U_alpha(p_3-p_2)(1-p_2),\n\n    Y_3=4a U_beta(z_2-z_3)(1-z_2),\n    Y_4=Y_3+4a U_beta(z_3-z_4)(1-z_3),\n    Y_1=Y_4+4a U_beta(z_4-z_1)(1-z_1).                   (17)\n\nHence0=X_1<=X_4<=X_3<=X_2 and0=Y_2<=Y_3<=Y_4<=Y_1.\nThe EXACT full all-value answer at all amplitudes and curvatures is\n\n    V_min=u^2 J_alpha+v^2 J_beta\n                  -min_(k in{1,4,3,2})(u^2 X_k+v^2 Y_k). (18)\n\nOn the strict open domain from Section1 with beta<1 and c,d>0, all\nfour candidates in the final minimum are positive for u,v>0. Thus the\njoint price is strictly larger than the sum of the separate scalar\nprices. The theorem below pays this actual positive price; it does not\npretend the coordinatewise minimizations are additive.\n\nThere is also an exact finite amplitude elimination. Normalize\nu^2+v^2=1 and put lambda=u^2. For fixed alpha,beta the right side is\nthe maximum of the four affine lines\n\n    L_k(lambda)=A_k+B_k lambda,\n    A_k=J_beta-Y_k,\n    B_k=(J_alpha-X_k)-(J_beta-Y_k).                       (19)\n\nIts minimum on[0,1] is attained at0,1, or a pairwise intersection\nlambda=(A_l-A_k)/(B_k-B_l) lying in[0,1], for B_k!=B_l. Evaluate\nthe maximum of ALL four lines at each candidate. Parallel or identical\nlines create no missing candidate: a flat minimum interval has an\nendpoint at an intersection or at0 or1. At most six intersections\nand the two endpoints suffice. This covers amplitude-zero boundaries,\nties, and collapsed branches without a numerical optimization.\n\n## 5. The retained slow mode pays the full price through node2\n\nThe quantitative estimate is\n\n                 J_alpha-X_2>=41 alpha^2/576.             (20)\n\nThis is uniform over every word and curvature in(1)--(2), independent\nof the other mode. The proof is given completely here.\n\nFirst observe from(16) that J_alpha-X_2 is the scalar energy obtained\nby using the route4->3->2->0 for sink0, the direct route4->1 for\nsink1, and the usual monotone routes for sinks2,3. This route choice\nis an UPPER bound on the scalar price, rather than its optimum.\nCall the corresponding algebraic energy E_tail. Its value is allowed\nto be below the scalar minimum; we prove even E_tail is positive.\n\nUse theta=alpha, ell=1-a*theta, m=ell*(b*theta-1). After the first\ntwo steps the scalar point is -m. Append a list of zero, one, or two\ntail steps, each between0 and a. Let T_tail be its sum, s_tail=a+b+T_tail,\nand let -t be its terminal point. For a tail index k let p_k be its\npoint just before that update, and T_k the tail sum up to and including\nthat update. Then direct telescoping gives\n\n E_tail=1-(1+s_tail*theta)^2 t^2\n       -2theta(1-theta)[a(1+m)+b*ell*(ell+t)\n                    +theta sum_(tail k)(a+T_k)h_k p_k^2]. (21)\n\nHere is a derivation retaining every term. For any scalar sampled\nvalues write f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2-A_i. Substitution in(6), followed by\nx_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i, gives\n\n    V=x_0^2-(x_end+s_tail*g_end)^2\n                                      -2sum h_i(A_i-A_end).\n\nFor the specified route prices A_i=U_theta(p_i^2+d_i), A_end=U_theta*t^2.\nFor a negative monotone tail,\n\n    p_i^2-t^2+d_i=2theta sum_(k>=i in tail)h_k p_k^2.\n\nFor index0 the same expression is2(1+m) plus the expression for\nindex2; for index1 it is2ell(ell+t). Collecting each tail update's\ncoefficient gives a+T_k. As2U_theta=theta(1-theta), these identities\nprove(21), with no restriction on zero tail steps.\n\nNow append one more tail step r in[0,a] at the old terminal point -y,\nwhere y>=0, and write S for the old total mass. The new terminal\nmagnitude is y(1-r*theta). Subtracting(21) before and after the step\ngives the EXACT increment\n\n E_new-E_old\n  =theta^2*r*y^2{\n      theta(S+r)[2(S+1)-theta*r*(S+r)]+2b(1-theta)}\n       +2b*ell*y*theta^2*r*(1-theta).                    (22)\n\nTo check the terminal-square part, use\n(1+(S+r)theta)(1-r theta)=1+S theta-r(S+r)theta^2.\nThe new summand in(21) is theta(S-b+r)r*y^2, and its b*ell term\nchanges by -b*ell*y*r*theta. These give exactly(22).\n\nAll terms in(22) are nonnegative. Indeed theta*r<=1, and\n\n    2(S+1)-theta*r*(S+r)>=S+2-r>0,\n\nbecause S>=a+b and r<=a. Also theta<1, ell,y,r>=0. Thus\nE_tail is bounded below by the corresponding two-step energy E_empty.\nThis is a proved energy increment, not an assumed prefix or suffix\nheredity property of primitive schedules.\n\nFor the two-step word(a,b), the routes in question are both direct\nfrom its terminal point -m. Expanding their two ordered interpolation\nresiduals, or setting T_tail=0 in(21), gives\n\n E_empty/theta^2\n  =a(2-a)-b(b-2)ell^2\n       -[2(1-theta)/theta^2]m\n       -[2(1-theta)/theta^2+2(a+b)/theta+(a+b)^2]m^2.      (23)\n\nAll estimates for(23) follow from the explicit parameter box, without\nany older necessary inequality. They are\n\n    (1-theta)/theta^2<=b(b-1)<=45/16,\n    a+b<=4,\n    2(1-theta)/theta^2+2(a+b)/theta+(a+b)^2\n                                           <=317/8<40,\n    ell<=1-a/b,\n    0<=m=(1-a theta)(b theta-1)<=(b-a)^2/(4ab).            (24)\n\nThe first bound uses theta>=1/b and the decreasing function\n(1-theta)/theta^2 on(0,1]. The last bound follows by completing the\nsquare in the concave quadratic in theta. Its right side increases\nwith b/a>=1.\n\nIf7/5<=a<=3/2, then\n\n    a(2-a)>=3/4, ell<=17/45<2/5,\n    m<=289/5040<1/16, b(b-2)<=9/16.\n\nTherefore(23) is at least\n\n    3/4-9/100-45/128-5/32=487/3200>41/576.               (25)\n\nIf3/2<=a<=7/4, then\n\n    a(2-a)>=7/16, ell<=1/3, m<=1/24.\n\nEquation(23) is now at least\n\n    7/16-1/16-15/64-5/72=41/576.                         (26)\n\nBoth ranges include their common boundary. Equations(22),(25),(26)\nprove(20) jointly, including m=0, ell=0, and terminal product zero.\n\n## 6. Full amplitude/curvature comparison with the complete scalar channel\n\nThe k=2 route in(18) has Y_2=0. Thus(18) and(20) imply the uniform\npointwise inequality\n\n    V_min>=u^2(J_alpha-X_2)+v^2 J_beta\n            >=(41/576)alpha^2*u^2+v^2 J_beta.             (27)\n\nLet E_mix(h) be the infimum of(18) over all alpha,beta in(2) and\nu^2+v^2=1. The infimum is attained: the parameter intervals and the\namplitude segment are compact and(18) is continuous. Define\n\n    j_min(h)=min_(1/a<=beta<=beta_max) J_beta(h).\n\nPure fast amplitudes u=0,v=1 attain J_beta. Equation(27) proves\n\n    min(0,E_mix(h))=min(0,j_min(h)),\n    E_mix(h)>=min(41/(576b^2),j_min(h)).                  (28)\n\nIndeed if j_min<0, every mixture has value at least j_min and the\npure fast minimizer attains it. If j_min>=0 every mixture is\nnonnegative. This eliminates ALL slow curvatures and ALL amplitudes\nfor the complete sign comparison, not merely one selected ratio.\n\nThere is no unresolved fast-curvature sign branch. Formula(13), with\nthe beta distances in(11), is a single explicit polynomial in beta\nof degree at most10 on the entire interval[1/a,beta_max]. Thus\nj_min is obtained exactly from the two endpoints and every real root\nof its derivative in the interval. If the derivative is identically\nzero, either endpoint gives the constant value. Equal endpoints and\nmultiple roots cause no omission. No polynomial root has been\nnumerically estimated here; this is an exact finite algebraic rule.\n\nFor precision about the older system, denote by S_scalar(h) the\nCOMPLETE value-optimized scalar channel: for every theta in[0,1],\nthe scalar samples q_i=product_(j<i)(1-theta h_j), gradients theta*q_i,\nand every jointly interpolable choice of their values must have V>=0.\nThis is the scalar channel used for the retained-mode comparison;\nbare quadratic values alone are a strictly different test definition.\nOnly its inequalities J_beta>=0 for beta in(2) are used here.\n\nLet N_old(h) denote the conjunction of ANY other old cut, triangle,\nreturn, curvature-repair, compact-box, or full-cone necessary tests,\nwith their whole original parameter domains retained. Then(28) gives\nthe exact logical comparison\n\n [N_old(h) and S_scalar(h) and nonnegativity of this entire joint family]\n                iff [N_old(h) and S_scalar(h)]             (29)\n\nthroughout(1). No old test is discarded or replaced. None of N_old is\nneeded to prove this implication. The only old necessary conditions\nactually USED are the displayed fully optimized scalar inequalities.\nThe bounds in(1) are explicit restrictions of the investigated family,\nnot claimed new necessary conditions on every four-step seed.\n\nThe distinction matters: this draft does NOT claim that a smaller\nfinite list using only unrepriced quadratics, cut tests, and C83 implies\nJ_beta>=0. No independence from that smaller list has been tested or\nasserted. The proved statement is complete dominance by actual scalar\nvalue-optimized witnesses, hence redundancy after the complete scalar\nchannel even when every other old condition is retained.\n\nIf this joint family supplies any negative-energy witness, (28) supplies\na pure scalar witness with at least as negative an energy under the\nsame amplitude normalization. This is an energy comparison; it is not\na comparison of normalized terminal objective payoffs after repricing.\n\n## 7. Boundaries and the original formal space\n\nNo zero-step compression is used. If c=0 or d=0 the corresponding\npoints and gradients coincide, the intervening costs vanish, and both\ndirected inequalities force equality of values. The original nodes\nand all twenty multipliers nevertheless remain in(7)--(18).\n\nAt alpha=1/b the slow mode dies at index2; at alpha=1/a it dies at\nindex1. At beta=1/a the fast mode dies at index1; at beta=1/c or1/d,\nwhen present, it dies at that tail update. Equations(4)--(28) retain\nevery such case. These boundaries do not change the fact that BOTH\nmodes are genuinely retained on the open domain in Section1.\n\nIf beta=1 then U_beta=0 and all Y_k vanish, so k=1 gives zero joint\nprice. If one amplitude vanishes, (18) reduces to the corresponding\nscalar optimum. Tied distances, tied prices, and equal curvature\nalpha=beta=1/a are included. No strict ordering is used in an endpoint\nproof, and no sign is divided out.\n\n## 8. One static attaining function, actual minimizers, and true distance\n\nTake the jointly minimizing values f_i=F_base(x_i)-D_i. On R^2 set\n\n    cjet_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i,\n    Conj_i(w)=cjet_i+<x_i,w-g_i>+||w-g_i||^2/2,\n    Conj(w)=max_(0<=i<=4)Conj_i(w),\n    F(x)=sup_w[<x,w>-Conj(w)].                            (30)\n\nThis construction uses every finite node, including terminal4, and\ndoes not force the base origin to remain a minimizer.\n\nFor a self-contained verification, Conj is finite, 1-strongly convex,\nand has quadratic growth. The supremum defining F has a unique\nmaximizer w(x). Its two strong-convexity inequalities imply\n\n    <x-x',w(x)-w(x')> >= ||w(x)-w(x')||^2.\n\nThus w is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the defining maxima at two nearby\npoints shows differentiability with gradient w: the difference between\nF(x+d)-F(x) and <w(x),d> is bounded in absolute value by ||d||^2.\nThe supremum representation gives convexity and finiteness.\n\nExpanding at g_i gives Conj_j(g_i)-cjet_i=Q_ij<=0. Equality holds\nfor j=i, so Conj(g_i)=cjet_i and x_i is a subgradient of Conj at g_i.\nConsequently F(x_i)=f_i and grad F(x_i)=g_i. This proves actual static\nattainment of all the joint values and the entire original GD word.\n\nDefine\n\n    A_i=Conj_i(0)=D_i-u^2 U_alpha p_i^2-v^2 U_beta z_i^2,\n    A_max=max_i A_i,  Active={i:A_i=A_max},\n    y_i=x_i-g_i=((1-alpha)u*p_i,(1-beta)v*z_i).\n\nThen the true minimizers and minimum value are\n\n    MinHull=argmin F=conv{y_i:i in Active},\n    min F=-A_max.                                        (31)\n\nTo prove this, the directional derivative of Conj at0 is\nmax_(i in Active)<y_i,w>. Its subgradient set is the displayed convex\nhull: the hull gives supporting inequalities, and strict separation\nrules out a vector outside the hull. By the unique-maximizer condition,\ngrad F(x)=0 iff x belongs to that subgradient set. This proves(31).\n\nFor u^2+v^2>0, x_0 lies outside MinHull. Equations(4)--(5) imply\np_i,z_i<=1 for every i, so\n\n    <x_0,x_0-y_i>\n      =u^2[1-(1-alpha)p_i]+v^2[1-(1-beta)z_i]\n      >=alpha*u^2+beta*v^2>0.                            (32)\n\nHence R_true=dist(x_0,MinHull)>0. An exact finite formula for R_true^2\nis the minimum of the distances to every active vertex and every\nsegment between active vertices. For distinct y_i,y_j use\n\n    tau_ij=min(1,max(0,<x_0-y_i,y_j-y_i>/||y_j-y_i||^2)),\n    distance_ij^2=||x_0-y_i-tau_ij(y_j-y_i)||^2.           (33)\n\nCoincident vertices use their vertex candidate. An exterior point's\nclosest point in a planar convex hull lies on a vertex or edge, and\nevery listed segment lies in that hull; this proves equality.\n\nSince D_4=0, the TRUE W-normalized terminal objective of this actual\nattaining function is\n\n    2[F_base(x_4)+A_max]/R_true^2.                        (34)\n\nTranslate a closest minimizer to0, subtract min F, and rescale by\nR_true to obtain an initial distance1 instance with exactly this\npayoff and the same word. Primitive energy changes only by the square\nof the length scale. Formula(28) does not claim a payoff comparison\nbetween the mixed and pure witnesses with their different minimizer\nsets. This prevents the fixed-origin error identified in the prior wave.\n\n## 9. A full-certificate member surviving EVERY old necessary test\n\nThis section proves the word domain is not one already excluded by\nthe complete older system. It does not assert a threshold survivor.\n\nLet a=sqrt(2), let delta be the unique positive solution of\n\n    delta(4+a+delta)=3(1+a),\n\nand set h=(a,1+delta,1,1). The increasing left side is smaller than\nthe right at delta=1, since its difference there is2-2a<0. At\ndelta=5/4 the difference is(57-28a)/16>0, since a<3/2.\nTherefore1<delta<5/4, and this word lies in(1). Its two retained\ncurvature intervals have nonempty interiors.\n\nSet\n\n    T_mass=4+a+delta, kappa=(T_mass/3)^2>1.\n\nUse the following original finite multipliers, with every other\noff-diagonal entry zero and every diagonal entry zero:\n\n    Lambda_01=2,           Lambda_10=2a+2,\n    Lambda_14=delta(kappa-1),\n    Lambda_21=kappa-1,     Lambda_23=2kappa,\n    Lambda_24=kappa-1,\n    Lambda_31=kappa-1,     Lambda_32=4kappa,\n    Lambda_34=9kappa-1,\n    Lambda_41=kappa-1,     Lambda_43=12kappa.              (35)\n\nAll twenty signs are satisfied. The exact full formal identity is\n\n    V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij\n        =kappa[||g_2-g_3||^2+4||g_3-g_4||^2].            (36)\n\nHere is a derivation from the accepted composition identity, including\nits child certificates. For a one-step word a=sqrt(2), direct expansion\ngives\n\n    V_(a)+2 Q_01+(2a+2)Q_10=0.                           (37)\n\nFor the two unit steps, temporarily on local indices0,1,2, direct\nsubstitution gives\n\n    V_(1,1)+2Q_10+4Q_21=||g_0||^2+3||g_1||^2-4||g_2||^2.\n\nFor a unit update Q_ij+Q_ji, with j=i+1, equals\n||g_j||^2-<g_i,g_j>. Its exact\nadded interpolation combination is\n\n    (kappa-1)[Q_21+Q_31+Q_41+delta Q_14+Q_24+Q_34].\n\nEmbedding the two proved child identities on the original nodes\n0,1 and2,3,4 gives exactly(35)--(36). For a flow audit, the join\nidentity (kappa-1)(3-delta)=2(1+a+delta) gives net flows\n(-2a,-2(1+delta),-2,-2,2(a+3+delta)), exactly omega.\nThe remainder in(36) is PSD on all five independent formal gradients;\nno quotient or rank restriction was imposed.\n\nConsequently every actual trajectory on this word has nonnegative\nprimitive energy. Every valid old necessary test, with all its\nparameters, passes; otherwise its negative actual witness would\ncontradict(36). This is stronger than checking a subset of old tests.\nThe same word still has a strictly positive joint price for the\ninterior curvatures and amplitudes described in Section4.\n\nIts capacity is T_mass<4+3/2+5/4=27/4<193/25, so(35) is expressly\nNOT the missing threshold seed. The shared result remains an\nunverified joint draft rather than an accepted new fact.\n\n## 10. UNVERIFIED;original target remains open.", "evidence": "# Two retained modes with a long step and every tail sign\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft, max2 round30, the second/final of\nthe TWO analytical rounds allocated after completed baseline28 by TASK.md\nand master_guidance4226374d0649bf55. No fact_submit is authorized or used.\nNothing new in this document is a verifier-accepted fact.\n\nAccepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraphs1--2, supplies the\nordered smooth-convex interpolation convention;04219554e41d9750,\nSections10--11, supplies the original primitive certificate identity and\nthe primitive composition identity. That member is below the\nrequired seed-capacity threshold.\n\nThe original goal and W=2R convention are unchanged. The result is an\nenergy-dominance statement for a restricted, explicitly stated family,\nnot a normalized objective comparison, a threshold seed, a global\nno-seed theorem, or a resolution of the unrestricted exact-profile Goal.\n\nNotation convention: every new local letter with an unsuffixed printed\nname below denotes its version with suffix30. This includes a,b,c,d,\nalpha,beta,u,v,p,z,ell,m,k,l,A_gap,B_gap,U,w,D,d_i^theta,B_theta,\nJ_theta,E_A,E_B,Delta,E_mix,j_min,rho,Conj,A_i,MinHull,R_true,t,delta,\nkappa,C_right and T_mass. These do not redefine older project symbols.\nThe original h,s,Q_ij,V_h,Lambda_ij,L_flow,D_prim,M_prim keep their\naccepted meanings. T_orig below is the original unit-diagonal prefix\nmatrix denoted T in accepted04219554e41d9750, not the unrelated project\nsingle-cut scalar T. All further local notation is defined at first use.\n\n## 1. The actual geometry and every sign branch\n\nFix\n\n    h=(a,b,c,d),\n    7/5<=a<=7/4,   2<b<=9/4,   0<=c,d<=a,                (1)\n    1/b<=alpha<=1/a,   1/a<=beta<=1,   u,v>=0.             (2)\n\nPut s=a+b+c+d and C=1+s. Initial coordinate reflections permit u,v>=0\nwithout losing an amplitude sign. Both zero-amplitude endpoints remain.\nFor i=0,...,4 define\n\n    p_i=product_(j<i)(1-alpha*h_j),\n    z_i=product_(j<i)(1-beta*h_j),\n    x_i=(u*p_i,v*z_i),\n    g_i=(alpha*u*p_i,beta*v*z_i).                         (3)\n\nThe empty products equal1. These are the exact iterates and gradients of\none static convex 1-smooth base function\n\n    F_base(x,y)=alpha*x^2/2+beta*y^2/2.\n\nThe recursion is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. No finite index is compressed.\n\nWrite\n\n    ell=1-a*alpha, m=ell*(b*alpha-1),\n    k=1-c*alpha, l=1-d*alpha.\n\nThen ell,m>=0, k,l in[0,1], and\n\n    p=(1,ell,-m,-m*k,-m*k*l),\n    p_2<=p_3<=p_4<=p_1<=p_0=1.                           (4)\n\nFor the fast coordinate put r=a*beta-1 and q=b*beta-1. Both are\nnonnegative, r<=3/4, and\n\n    z=(1,-r,r*q,r*q*(1-c*beta),\n                         r*q*(1-c*beta)*(1-d*beta)).      (5)\n\nThe function (a*beta-1)(b*beta-1) increases for beta>=1/a, because\nits derivative is2ab*beta-a-b>=b-a>0. Consequently\n\n    0<=z_2<= (a-1)(b-1)<=15/16<1.\n\nBoth tail multipliers lie in[-r,1]. Thus z_3,z_4<=z_2. If z_3<0,\nthen |z_3|<=r*z_2<=r. If z_4<0, precisely one of the two tail\nmultipliers is negative, so |z_4|<=r*z_2<=r. It follows that\n\n    z_1<=z_3,z_4<=z_2<z_0=1.                             (6)\n\nOnly the order of nodes3 and4 varies:\n\n    c*beta<=1:    z_1<=z_4<=z_3<=z_2<z_0,  branch A;\n    c*beta>=1:    z_1<=z_3<=z_4<=z_2<z_0,  branch B.       (7)\n\nIndeed z_4-z_3=-d*beta*z_3. The sign of d*beta-1 changes the\nterminal sign but introduces no additional spatial-order branch.\nAt c*beta=1 both z_3 and z_4 vanish, and the two displayed orders\nagree with ties.\n\nFor alpha strictly between its endpoints, u,v>0, and c,d<a, the slow\nmode is retained with signs +,+,-,-,-. In branch B with c*beta>1\nand d*beta>1, the fast signs are +,-,+,-,+ and the terminal is\nnonzero. This open parameter set is nonempty, including at the\nfull-certificate word constructed in Section9. All endpoint and\nproduct-zero cases in(1)--(7) remain part of the result.\n\n## 2. Original primitive interface and the terminal-source price\n\nFor ALL distinct i,j in{0,1,2,3,4}, impose\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.\n\nThe energy is exactly\n\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)\n          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_4||^2.     (8)\n\nThe full original certificate uses all twenty Lambda_ij>=0, with\nzero diagonal, and\n\n    e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T,\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2a,-2b,-2c,-2d,2s)^T,\n    (T_orig)_ii=1, (T_orig)_it=h_t for t<i, zero for t>i,\n    D_prim=diag(a(2-a),b(2-b),c(2-c),d(2-d),-s(s+2)).\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 proves the exact implication and identity\n\n    L_flow^T e=omega,\n    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow*T_orig+T_orig^T*L_flow^T)/2,\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                          =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>.   (9)\n\nA full seed requires M_prim PSD on ALL of R^5. Neither the two-mode\ntest geometry nor an optimal price flow replaces that requirement.\n\nPut U_theta=theta(1-theta)/2 for theta=alpha,beta. At the base values,\ndirect substitution gives symmetric nonnegative slacks\n\n    w_ij=-Q_ij(F_base)\n        =u^2 U_alpha(p_i-p_j)^2+v^2 U_beta(z_i-z_j)^2.    (10)\n\nFor f_i=F_base(x_i)+epsilon_i the twenty inequalities are exactly\nepsilon_i-epsilon_j<=w_ij. Let D_i be the shortest w-cost from the\nORIGINAL terminal source4 to i, with D_4=0. Nonnegative weights allow\ncycles to be removed, so an attaining simple path exists even with\nzero-cost edges. Both directed inequalities on each edge are present.\nFollowing a path from4 to i gives epsilon_i-epsilon_4>=-D_i.\nSince h_i>=0, the simultaneous all-value minimum therefore is\n\n    V_min=u^2 B_alpha+v^2 B_beta\n                                  -2sum_(i<4)h_i D_i,   (11)\n\nwhere B_theta is the base scalar energy defined in(15) below. It is\nattained at epsilon_i=-D_i: D_j<=D_i+w_ij proves every required\nepsilon_i-epsilon_j<=w_ij.\n\nFor the dual orientation, route2h_i units from4 to each i<4 along\nan attaining path. This has L_flow^T e=omega and cost2sum h_i D_i.\nConversely w_ij>=D_j-D_i implies that any nonnegative flow with this\nsame divergence costs at least2sum h_i D_i. This establishes the\nexact terminal-source price. It does not assert PSD of that auxiliary\nflow's original M_prim.\n\nSection8 proves that the minimizing values come from an actual static\nsmooth convex function with a nonempty minimizer set. Thus no finite\nvalue solution used here is an unattained relaxation.\n\n## 3. Exact full joint distances: six routes, with all ties retained\n\nThe complete answer on the WHOLE parameter domain is\n\n    D_4=0,\n    D_3=w_43,\n    D_1=min(w_41,w_43+w_31),\n    D_2=min(w_42,w_43+w_32),                              (12)\n    D_0=min(w_40,\n            w_43+w_30,\n            w_41+w_10,\n            w_43+w_31+w_10,\n            w_42+w_20,\n            w_43+w_32+w_20).\n\nHere is a proof covering every simple path, not merely selected ones.\nFor any real coordinate values t_i and a nonnegative weight U,\n\n    U[(t_i-t_k)^2+(t_k-t_j)^2-(t_i-t_j)^2]\n                              =2U(t_k-t_i)(t_k-t_j).     (13)\n\nIf node k lies outside the coordinate interval between i,j in BOTH\ncoordinates, removing an internal visit to k cannot increase the cost.\nNode0 is a coordinatewise maximum by(4),(6), so it can be removed from\nevery path between core nodes{1,2,3,4}. Within the core, node1 is a\nslow maximum and fast minimum; node2 is a slow minimum and fast maximum.\nEither can therefore be removed whenever it occurs internally in a\ncore path. Hence the only routes to1 are4->1 or4->3->1 after such\nshortcuts; the only routes to2 are4->2 or4->3->2; and the direct\nroute4->3 is optimal. This proves the first four lines of(12).\nFor a shortest path ending at0, its last core node is one of1,2,3,4.\nReplace its prefix by an optimal path in the core. These possibilities\nare exactly the six last-line candidates. All shortcuts are weak\ninequalities, so ties, coincident points, and zero coordinate weights\ncause no exception. Every listed route retains original node labels.\n\nFor fixed alpha,beta there is an exact finite amplitude elimination.\nNormalize u^2+v^2=1 and set rho=u^2 in[0,1]. Every route cost in(12)\nis affine in rho. Equation(11) is a continuous convex piecewise affine\nfunction: it is an affine function minus nonnegative multiples of\nthe minima in(12). Its minimum on[0,1] is attained at an endpoint or\nat an intersection of two distinct affine route costs from the SAME\nminimum. There are at most15 such pairs for D_0, one for D_1 and one\nfor D_2, hence at most19 candidate locations including0 and1. Retain\nonly intersections in[0,1] with nonzero slope difference; evaluate the\nFULL expression(11) at each. Parallel/identical lines cause no gap:\na flat minimizing interval has a boundary breakpoint or endpoint.\nInactive intersections merely give harmless extra candidate locations.\n\n## 4. All-value scalar energies and both curvature branches\n\nFor real sampled coordinates, the shortest squared-distance path\nbetween two samples uses every distinct intermediate sampled value in\nmonotone order. To prove this, list the nonnegative gaps between\nconsecutive distinct sorted values. The square of an edge spanning\nseveral gaps is at least the sum of their squares. Every path between\nthe endpoints crosses each intervening gap at least once. Summing gives\nthe lower bound, attained by the monotone path. Ties add zero gaps.\n\nThe unweighted slow squared-distance minima from terminal4 are\n\n    d_0^alpha=(1-p_1)^2+(p_1-p_4)^2,\n    d_1^alpha=(p_1-p_4)^2,\n    d_2^alpha=(p_2-p_3)^2+(p_3-p_4)^2,\n    d_3^alpha=(p_3-p_4)^2,   d_4^alpha=0.                (14)\n\nFor any curvature theta, writing q_i=product_(j<i)(1-theta*h_j),\ndirect substitution into(8) gives the unit-amplitude base energy\n\n    B_theta=theta sum_(i<4)h_i[1+theta(1-h_i)]q_i^2\n                         -s*theta[1+C*theta]q_4^2.      (15)\n\nThe exact scalar value minimum is\n\n    J_theta=B_theta-theta(1-theta)sum_(i<4)h_i d_i^theta. (16)\n\nFor the fast coordinate the COMPLETE formulas are:\n\n    branch A (c*beta<=1):\n    d_0^beta=(1-z_2)^2+(z_2-z_3)^2+(z_3-z_4)^2,\n    d_1^beta=(z_1-z_4)^2,\n    d_2^beta=(z_2-z_3)^2+(z_3-z_4)^2,\n    d_3^beta=(z_3-z_4)^2;\n\n    branch B (c*beta>=1):\n    d_0^beta=(1-z_2)^2+(z_2-z_4)^2,\n    d_1^beta=(z_1-z_3)^2+(z_3-z_4)^2,\n    d_2^beta=(z_2-z_4)^2,\n    d_3^beta=(z_3-z_4)^2.                               (17)\n\nIn both branches d_4^beta=0. They agree at c*beta=1. No branch is\ndiscarded when d*beta passes through1. Equations(11),(14)--(17),\napplied in one dimension, and the construction in Section8 prove\nactual attainment for all scalar minima J_theta. These are ALL-value\nscalar tests, not merely the energies of the original quadratic values.\n\nFor u,v>0, alpha<1, beta<1, m>0, k>0, d>0, and c*beta>1,\nthere is a genuine strictly positive joint price. For example, scalar\noptimal routes to1 are direct for the slow coordinate and via3 for\nthe fast coordinate. The extra cost of the two respective joint routes\nis either\n\n    2v^2 U_beta(z_4-z_3)(z_3-z_1),\n    or 2u^2 U_alpha(p_4-p_3)(p_1-p_3),\n\nboth strictly positive under these strict conditions. Formula(12)\nproves their minimum is the excess for D_1. Thus the coming dominance\nproof pays an actual joint price; it does not assume scalar additivity.\n\n## 5. A complete positive slow-coordinate budget\n\nWe prove, jointly here, that using scalar-fast-optimal routes for every\nsink leaves at least alpha^2/40 of slow energy. The calculation has\ntwo parts: a slow budget independent of fast signs, then the cost of\nthe sole fast-order swap.\n\nFirst take these routes, denoted route system A:\n\n    sink0:4->3->2->0; sink1:4->1;\n    sink2:4->3->2;    sink3:4->3.                         (18)\n\nThey are fast-optimal throughout branch A by(7). Let E_A denote the\nslow scalar base energy minus its price on exactly these routes. We\nwill prove\n\n    E_A/alpha^2 >=487/3200 if a<=3/2,\n    E_A/alpha^2 >=41/576   if a>=3/2.                    (19)\n\nThese designated-route energies are algebraic lower bounds, not\nasserted feasible scalar value assignments. A designated route can\ncost more than a scalar shortest path. This only strengthens the\nrequired estimate: proving that its remaining energy is positive\nalso proves positivity after the smaller true scalar price.\n\nUse theta=alpha. Start with the first two steps(a,b), whose terminal\npoint is -m, and append zero, one or two monotone slow tail steps\nbetween0 and a. Write T_tail for their total, S=a+b+T_tail for the\nwhole current sum, and -y for the current terminal point. For a tail\nstep indexed j, write T_j for the tail sum up to and including that\nstep and q_j for its scalar point before the update. The corresponding\nroute system always sends sink0 through all tail nodes in reverse\nchronology to2 then0, sends sink1 directly, and uses monotone tail\npaths for tail sinks. Its energy is\n\n    E_tail=1-(1+S*theta)^2 y^2\n      -2theta(1-theta)[a(1+m)+b*ell*(ell+y)\n               +theta sum_(tail j)(a+T_j)h_j q_j^2].     (20)\n\nHere is the full telescoping derivation. For arbitrary scalar sampled\nvalues put Acal_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2-f_i. Substituting this definition\nin(8) and using x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i gives the exact identity\n\n    V=x_0^2-(x_end+S*g_end)^2\n                              -2sum h_i(Acal_i-Acal_end).\n\nFor values priced along the designated routes,\nAcal_i=U_theta(q_i^2+d_i), where d_i is that route's unweighted\nsquared cost, and Acal_end=U_theta*y^2. On the negative monotone tail,\n\n    q_i^2-y^2+d_i=2theta sum_(j>=i in tail)h_j q_j^2.\n\nThis follows term by term from\nq_j^2-q_(j+1)^2+(q_j-q_(j+1))^2=2theta h_j q_j^2.\nFor sink0 the expression is2(1+m) plus the expression for sink2;\nfor sink1 it is2ell(ell+y). Collecting the coefficient of each tail\nstep yields a+T_j and proves(20).\n\nAppend a tail step t in[0,a] to an old terminal -y and old sum S.\nThe new magnitude is y(1-t*theta). Subtracting(20) at the two lengths\ngives exactly\n\n    E_new-E_old\n      =theta^2*t*y^2{theta(S+t)[2(S+1)-theta*t*(S+t)]\n                                      +2b(1-theta)}\n        +2b*ell*y*theta^2*t*(1-theta). Static attainment, actual minimizers, and true initial distance\n\nSet f_i=F_base(x_i)-D_i with D_i given by(12), and on R^2 define\n\n    cjet_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i,\n    Conj_i(w)=cjet_i+<x_i,w-g_i>+||w-g_i||^2/2,\n    Conj(w)=max_(0<=i<=4)Conj_i(w),\n    F(x)=sup_w[<x,w>-Conj(w)].                           (33)\n\nEvery original finite node, including the terminal, occurs. The base\norigin is NOT required to remain a minimizer.\n\nFor completeness, Conj is finite, continuous, 1-strongly convex and\nhas quadratic growth, so the supremum in(33) has a unique maximizer\nw(x). {"id": "221c2d1202a35074", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T07:56:11.653763+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "max59 VERIFIED and persisted local primitive-certificate capacity theorem: fact a23356adcbb96c7e. Original all-word Goal remains OPEN.", "evidence": "Accepted fact a23356adcbb96c7e establishes the following complete statement:\n\nStrict local maximality of full primitive-certificate capacity at the balanced seven-step word.\n\nAll vectors and matrices are real. Put q58=sqrt(2), lambda=1+q58, u=q58-1=1/lambda,\n    hbar=(q58,2,q58,1+lambda,q58,2,q58), Abar=lambda^3.\nIndices of the seven physical steps run from0 to6; indices of coefficient arrays run from0 to7. Let e be the all-ones column in R^8. For h=(h0,...,h6) in[0,infinity)^7 set s=sum_(i=0)^6 h_i and A(h)=1+s. For a real zero-diagonal8-by8 array X set L(X)=diag(Xe)-X. Define the8-by8 matrix T(h) by T(h)_ii=1, T(h)_ij=h_j for j<i, and T(h)_ij=0 for j>i. For a square real matrix K let sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2. Set\n    omega(h)=(-2h0,...,-2h6,2s)^T,\n    D(h)=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h6(2-h6),-s(s+2)),\n    M(h,Lambda)=D(h)+sym(L(Lambda)T(h)).\nCall (h,Lambda) feasible if Lambda is a real8-by8 array with Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for every one of the56 ordered distinct pairs, L(Lambda)^T e=omega(h), and M(h,Lambda) is positive semidefinite on the entire R^8. No symmetry, support, rank, zero-remainder, or equality between formal gradient coordinates is assumed. Nonzero singular positive semidefinite remainders are included.\n\nDefine Lambdabar by its following twenty entries, with every other entry equal to zero:\n    01:2; 10:2lambda; 13:2lambda;\n    21:2lambda*q58; 23:2lambda^2+2lambda*q58; 31:2lambda;\n    32:2lambda^3; 37:2lambda^2; 43:2lambda*q58;\n    45:2lambda^2; 47:2lambda*q58; 53:4lambda;\n    54:2lambda^3; 57:2lambda^3+4lambda; 63:2lambda*q58;\n    65:2lambda^3*q58; 67:2lambda^4+2lambda^3*q58+2lambda*q58;\n    73:2lambda; 75:2lambda^3; 76:2lambda^5.\nHere ij denotes row i, column j. Let E58 be these twenty ordered pairs and Z0 the other36 ordered distinct pairs. The pair(hbar,Lambdabar) is feasible and M(hbar,Lambdabar)=0.\n\nFor p in R^7 and a real zero-diagonal8-by8 array Y, let S=sum_(i=0)^6 p_i, let Tp have entry p_j for j<i and zero elsewhere, and put\n    op=(-2p0,...,-2p6,2S)^T,\n    D1(p)=diag(2(1-hbar_i)p_i for i<7,-2Abar*S),\n    W(p,Y)=D1(p)+sym(L(Lambdabar)Tp+L(Y)T(hbar)),\n    f(p,Y)=L(Y)^T e-op.\nThe symbol f(p,Y) denotes this vector-valued flow defect, distinct from scalar objective values in the proof. Define\n    C58={(p,Y): f(p,Y)=0, Y_ij>=0 for ij in Z0, W(p,Y) is positive semidefinite on R^8}.\nThe entries of Y on E58 and all components of p are unrestricted real numbers in this definition. Use the Euclidean norm on physical vectors and the Frobenius norm on arrays, and write\n    ||(p,Y)||_* = sqrt(||p||_2^2+||Y||_F^2).\n\nThe following intermediate conclusions hold jointly. Every(p,Y) in C58 has S<=0, and S=0 in C58 holds if and only if p=0 and Y=0. Consequently Lambdabar is the unique feasible coefficient array at h=hbar. Every feasible pair with h in[1,4]^7 satisfies sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<=2^24. For every sequence of feasible pairs(h_k,Lambda_k) with h_k tending to hbar, the entire sequence Lambda_k tends to Lambdabar; thus this convergence holds uniformly over arbitrary choices of feasible arrays.\n\nFor arbitrary displacements p,Y, the exact full-matrix expansion is\n    M(hbar+p,Lambdabar+Y)=W(p,Y)+R2(p,Y),\n    R2(p,Y)=diag(-p0^2,...,-p6^2,-S^2)+sym(L(Y)Tp),\n    ||R2(p,Y)||_F<=13||(p,Y)||_*^2.\nDefine K58={(p,Y) in C58:||(p,Y)||_*=1}. This set is nonempty and compact, and\n    m58=min_((p,Y) in K58)(-sum_(i=0)^6 p_i)>0.\n\nWith c=m58/2, there exists epsilon>0 such that for EVERY h in[0,infinity)^7 and EVERY full feasible array Lambda at h, the condition ||h-hbar||_2<epsilon implies\n    A(h)<=lambda^3-c*sqrt(||h-hbar||_2^2+||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F^2).\nIn particular, within this physical neighborhood capacity equals lambda^3 if and only if(h,Lambda)=(hbar,Lambdabar), and every such feasible array satisfies\n    ||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F<=(sqrt(7)/c)||h-hbar||_2.\nOnly h is required epsilon-close in the hypothesis; there is no hypothesis restricting coefficient displacement or selecting a particular certificate. The coefficient estimate is relative to the base, with no assertion of pairwise Lipschitz selection or uniqueness away from the base. No numerical neighborhood radius, global capacity ceiling, or local/global gradient-descent objective minimax optimality is asserted. It is UNVERIFIED and is not an accepted predecessor.\nAll construction, smoothness, trajectory, local-bound and radius ingredients\nused here are proved jointly below. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used\nONLY for the original benchmark ray limit and its stated enclosure.\n\n## 1. The resulting theorem and its exact scope\n\nPut lambda=1+sqrt(2), d=lambda-2=1/lambda. For integer m>=32 and\n\n    1/5 <= tau <= 1/4,       8/m <= beta <= 1/4,\n\nuse the SAME enclosing parameters throughout the physical word:\n\n    eta_m=1-tau/m, c_m=lambda(1+tau/m), a_m=eta_m/c_m,\n    b_j=1+a_m c_m^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1, J=m-2, L=m-1.             (1.1)\n\nHere beta is beta_arc, not eta_m. Select ONLY the first occurrences of\nlevels m-2 and m-1. The entire physical word is exactly\n\n    H_m=(H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1)).                (1.2)\n\nUse the direct circular construction in Sections2--4, unchanged from the\nfrozen candidate. This is an actual convex1-smooth function F on R^3,\ninitialized at e_1, and all steps are physical GD steps x^+=x-h grad F.\nWrite E_m(tau,beta)=2F(x_(n_m))/dist(e_1,argmin F)^2.\n\nThere is one finite integer m_0 such that for EVERY m>=m_0 and EVERY\n(tau,beta) in the displayed rectangle, the two old circular components\nare both exactly zero at the actual FINAL iterate. In fact the old pair\nenters its moving flat interval by the end of H_(m-1) and thereafter is\nstationary. No tail step, old component or radius term is discarded.\nConsequently its full actual payoff is exactly\n\n    E_m(tau,beta)=D_3^2/[2(1+S_(m-1)) R_true^2],      (1.3)\n\nwith D_3 and R_true defined in Sections3--4.\n\nLet B_n be the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark in Section8, and let the\npositive finite Z_1=lim_m B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m be supplied by accepted\nfact04219554e41d9750. Set C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta). Uniformly on the\nsame moving parameter rectangle,\n\n    B_(n_m) E_m(tau,beta)\n       - Z_1 exp(-tau)/(2 lambda C_beta^4) -> 0.      (1.4)\n\nIn particular, for every FIXED beta in(0,1/4],\n\n    lim_m B_(n_m) E_m(1/4,beta)\n       =Z_1 exp(-1/4)/(2 lambda C_beta^4)<7/10.       (1.5)\n\nThe same argument controls the explicitly specified nonfixed scaling\nbeta_m=8/m. More precisely,\n\n    lim_m sup_(8/m<=beta<=1/4) B_(n_m) E_m(1/4,beta)\n       =Z_1 exp(-1/4)/(2 lambda)<7/10.               (1.6)\n\nThus the supremum in(1.6) is below7/10 for every sufficiently large m.\nOver the full tau rectangle the corresponding limit of the supremum is\nZ_1 exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)<3/4, also giving an eventual uniform deficit.\n\nThese are upper bounds on this ONE unchanged three-coordinate witness\nfamily. They are NOT upper bounds on W_n, and do not settle the original\nall-n/all-nonnegative-word minimax question. They do not control beta<8/m\n(for example beta=1/m), other checkpoint selections or counts, or other\nstatic functions. The smaller-beta region remains open in this report.\nNo positive pre-tail limit has been added to the terminal payoff.\n\n## 2. The actual circular body and finite local construction\n\nFix beta in(0,1/4]. Define\n\n    R_circ=1+beta, C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta),\n    p(q)=beta+sqrt(R_circ^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,\n    v(q)=(p(q),-q),\n    K_ctr=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).           (2.1)\n\nThe circular center is(beta,beta); its disk contains0 because\n2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. Thus its radial normals support this convex hull\non the displayed arc. The endpoints are(C_beta,0) and(beta,-1).\nFor every(p,-q) in K_ctr,\n\n    0<=p<=C_beta, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta*q,\n    (p,0) belongs to K_ctr.                         (2.2)\n\nFor any compact convex K containing0 put\n\n    E_K(r)=max_(g in K){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}.             (2.3)\n\nCompleting the square identifies its unique maximizer as the Euclidean\nprojection Pi_K(r). Its characterization is\n\n    <r-Pi_K(r),g-Pi_K(r)> <=0 for every g in K.       (2.4)\n\nAdding this inequality at two points shows\n||Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s)||^2<=<Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s),r-s>.\nThe projection is therefore1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing\nexpressions in(2.3) gives the derivative Pi_K(r) and a remainder bounded\nby ||s-r||^2. Hence E_K is convex, nonnegative, differentiable and1-smooth.\nIts zero set is the polar cone of K: positive support in a direction\nproduces a positive value using a sufficiently small multiple of that\nsupporting vector, while nonpositive support makes0 the maximizer.\nThe cone of K_ctr is generated by its two nonzero endpoints, since\n\n    (p,-q)=((p-beta*q)/C_beta)(C_beta,0)+q(beta,-1).\n\nFor sigma>0 it follows that\n\n    E_(sigma K_ctr)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U<=V.   (2.5)\n\nUse the one-sided Huber function\n\n    Hub_delta(z)=0 for z<=0;\n                 z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta;\n                 delta*z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta.    (2.6)\n\nIt is convex and1-smooth, with derivative min(delta,max(z,0)).\n\nHere is a finite construction for the ACTUAL ordinary block H_J, with\nits ordered steps h_1,...,h_N, N=2^J-1. Set alpha_i=h_i/2. Construct\nr_i=(u_i,-y_i) backward from\n\n    r_N=(beta,-1), q_N=1.\n\nFor each i from N down to1 choose the unique q_(i-1) in[0,1) satisfying\n\n    (q_(i-1)+beta)/(p(q_(i-1))-beta)\n       =max{ beta/(C_beta-beta),\n               [y_i+(1-alpha_i)beta]\n                    /[u_i-(1-alpha_i)beta] },      (2.7)\n\nand set\n\n    r_(i-1)=r_i+alpha_i v(q_(i-1)).                 (2.8)\n\nAll our physical steps are positive. The denominator in(2.7) is positive\nbecause u_i>=beta and alpha_i>0. Its left side is continuous and strictly\nincreasing from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity. Thus every term is defined.\nThe symbols specify a finite mathematical recursion; it was not executed.\n\nFor clarity, the projection checks underlying this construction are as\nfollows. Inductively u_i>=beta and y_i>=1, and the updates increase both\nwhen read backward. At an interior projected arc point the centered ray\nhas ratio(y_i+beta)/(u_i-beta). Subtracting alpha_i*beta from its numerator\nand adding alpha_i*beta to its denominator can only decrease that ratio.\nThus(2.7) gives q_(i-1)<=q_i. At q_i=1 this upper bound is immediate. At\nthe capped endpoint q_i=0 the supporting condition is\n\n    y_i<=beta(u_i-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta).             (2.9)\n\nIt makes the second entry in the maximum in(2.7) no greater than the first,\nso q_(i-1)=0 as well. At an uncapped new point, (2.7)--(2.8) place r_(i-1)\non the outward radial ray of v(q_(i-1)): the cross-product condition is\nexactly(2.7), and y_(i-1)>=1>=q_(i-1) gives a radial factor at least1.\nThe circular normal then proves(2.4). At the capped new point, the maximum\ncondition instead gives(2.9) for i-1. The displacement from(C_beta,0) is a\nnonnegative combination of the circular endpoint normal and(0,1), which\nalso proves(2.4). The terminal r_N is on K_ctr. We have proved\n\n    Pi_(K_ctr)(r_i)=v(q_i),\n    u_i>=beta, 1<=y_i<=y_0, 0<=q_i<=1.             (2.10)\n\nIn particular, with u=u_0, y=y_0 and S=S_J,\n\n    u=beta+sum_i alpha_i p(q_(i-1)),\n    y=1+sum_i alpha_i q_(i-1),\n    u>=beta*y, M=u+y/beta>0.                       (2.11)\n\nThe inequality is termwise p>=beta*q. For a general incoming threshold\nell>=0 and amplitude D>0 define\n\n    sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y,\n    A=ell+ell_out/beta,\n    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K_ctr)(X-A,Y-ell_out).        (2.12)\n\nThis is one fixed finite smooth convex function, zero whenever X<=ell\nand Y>=0 by(2.5). Also its OUTPUT derivative is zero whenever Y>=ell_out,\nfor every X. Indeed replacing a gradient(p,-q) by(p,0) increases the\nmaximized expression by q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2 if q>0.\n\nThe physical ordinary states are\n\n    (X_i,Y_i)=(A+sigma*u_i,ell_out-sigma*y_i).       (2.13)\n\nThey start at(ell+D,0), and(2.8)--(2.10) show that each physical step h_i\nupdates them by -(h_i/2)grad Phi. Each query has X_i>ell and\n0<=Y_i<=ell_out-sigma<ell_out. The final local gradient is\nsigma(beta,-1). A physical checkpoint b>2, with T=b/2-1, sends it to\n\n    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*sigma*T,ell_out+sigma*T).      (2.14)\n\nThis is an exact half-step identity for the original physical b. It is\nin the zero set(2.5). Nothing here substitutes a shortened physical word.\n\n## 3. One static global function, every step, and its radius\n\nFor our identical ordinary blocks H_J, use the SAME u,y,M in both.\nPut\n\n    T_1=b_J/2-1, T_2=b_(J+1)/2-1,\n    sigma_1=1/M, D_2=T_1/M,\n    sigma_2=D_2/M, D_3=T_1*T_2/M^2,\n    ell_2=y/M, ell_3=D_2*y/M,\n    A_1=ell_2/beta, A_2=ell_2+ell_3/beta,\n    delta=D_3/(1+S_L).                            (3.1)\n\nFor sufficiently large m the two checkpoints exceed2; all the parameters\nare then positive. Define, before starting GD,\n\n    Phi_1(X,Y)=E_(sigma_1 K_ctr)(X-A_1,Y-ell_2),\n    Phi_2(Y,Z)=E_(sigma_2 K_ctr)(Y-A_2,Z-ell_3),\n    F(X,Y,Z)=(Phi_1(X,Y)+Phi_2(Y,Z)\n                                      +Hub_delta(Z-ell_3))/2. (3.2)\n\nThe function is convex and1-smooth. To check the constant, the linear map\nstacking(X,Y),(Y,Z),Z has norm sqrt(2), since its squared output norm is\nX^2+2Y^2+2Z^2. The separable sum of the three1-smooth functions has gradient\nLipschitz constant1 on the product space. Composition and the factor1/2\ntherefore give at most(1/2)*2=1. Every summand is nonnegative and F(0)=0.\n\nAt any point write the two projections as(P_1,-Q_1),(P_2,-Q_2), and put\nP_3=min(delta,max(Z-ell_3,0)). The exact full update is\n\n    X^+=X-h P_1/2,\n    Y^+=Y-h(P_2-Q_1)/2,\n    Z^+=Z-h(P_3-Q_2)/2.                            (3.3)\n\nAll components in this formula belong to the SAME static function.\nDuring the first H_J and b_J the first pair follows(2.13)--(2.14);\nits output is at most ell_2 before the checkpoint. The next component\nand final Huber are zero there, so they do not change these updates.\nThe first checkpoint gives\n\n    X=A_1-beta*D_2, Y=ell_2+D_2, Z=0.\n\nDuring the next H_J and its root checkpoint b_(J+1), the second pair\nfollows(2.13)--(2.14), with ell=ell_2,D=D_2. The first component stays\nzero in the second ordinary block: its input is fixed at or below A_1,\nand its output is at least ell_2. In particular Q_1=0 at every active\nquery. The final Huber is inactive while the third coordinate is below\nell_3. Therefore the actual state just after the root checkpoint is\n\n    v_in=A_1-beta*D_2,\n    w_in=A_2-beta*D_3,\n    Z_in=ell_3+D_3.                                (3.4)\n\nThis derives the incoming data without importing an unverified trajectory.\n\nThe ENTIRE minimum set is the simultaneous zero set of the summands:\n\n    X<=A_1, Y>=beta*X,\n    Y<=A_2, Z>=beta*(Y-ell_2),\n    Z<=ell_3.                                     (3.5)\n\nHere(2.5) and beta*A_1=ell_2 were used. From(2.11),\n\n    1-A_1=sigma_1*u, ell_2=sigma_1*y,\n    0<A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).                           (3.6)\n\nThe point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0) belongs to(3.5). For any point of(3.5),\nif X<0 its squared distance from e_1 is at least1. For 0<=X<=A_1,\nits squared distance is at least(1-X)^2+beta^2 X^2, because Y>=beta X.\nThis quadratic decreases until X=1/(1+beta^2), so it is minimized on\n[0,A_1] at A_1. The displayed x_star attains this bound, which is at most1.\nThus the TRUE closest-minimizer squared radius is exactly\n\n    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2\n            =sigma_1^2(u^2+y^2)>0.                 (3.7)\n\nThis uses every constraint in the actual minimum set. The radius has not\nbeen normalized using a freely chosen more distant minimizer.\n\nEvery smooth-convex interpolation row follows from this actual function.\nFor completeness, subtract <grad F(y),z> from F(z). Its minimum is attained\nat y. The1-smooth descent bound at x with displacement\n-(grad F(x)-grad F(y)) then yields\nF(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y> >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.\nThis holds for every ordered pair, including x_star and the terminal sample.\nActual Euclidean Gram matrices are positive semidefinite.\nFor general smoothness L_smooth>0, take f=L_smooth F and physical updates\nx^+=x-(h/L_smooth)grad f; the same trajectory and normalized payoff result.\n\n## 4. Exact tail dynamics and all terminal terms\n\nThroughout the entire remaining H_L the third-coordinate excess is\n\n    Z-ell_3=D_3-s'*delta/2>=delta,\n                         0<=s'<=S_L,              (4.1)\n\nwhere s' is the physical mass already traversed. This statement is an\nexact trajectory identity, justified inductively: it keeps Q_2=0 by the\noutput-isolation property in Section2, so P_3=delta and the displayed\nupdate continues. In particular the old second component has the exact\nhorizontal expression\n\n    Phi_2(w,Z)=Hub_(C_beta*sigma_2)(w-A_2).          (4.2)\n\nThe first coordinate never increases by(3.3), and hence v<=A_1. For any\nsuch v its projection lies on the sloped segment p=beta*q: for fixed q,\nreducing p to beta*q increases the expression being maximized, since\nv-A_1<=0 and p>=0. The segment is in K_ctr. Maximization along it gives\n\n    Phi_1(v,w)=Hub_((1+beta^2)*sigma_1)(beta*v-w)/(1+beta^2). (4.3)\n\nPut sigma=sigma_1, kappa_tail=1+beta^2, and cap_ratio=C_beta*D_2.\nThe exact dynamics for EVERY physical tail step h are therefore\n\n    q=min(sigma,max(beta*v-w,0)/kappa_tail),\n    r=min(cap_ratio*sigma,max(w-A_2,0)),\n    v^+=v-h*beta*q/2,\n    w^+=w+h*(q-r)/2.                               (4.4)\n\nThere is a closed flat interval beta*v<=w<=A_2, with actual width\n\n    g=A_2-beta*v,\n    g_in=ell_3/beta+beta^2*D_2>0,\n    g^+=g+h*beta^2*q/2>=g.                         (4.5)\n\nThus q and r cannot be positive together. When the pair enters that\ninterval both vanish, the pair is stationary, and(4.1) keeps it decoupled\nfrom the still evolving third coordinate. This is exact absorption.\n\nAt the final point the ENTIRE payoff is\n\n    E_m=[Phi_1(v_fin,w_fin)+Phi_2(w_fin,Z_fin)\n                           +D_3^2/(2(1+S_L))]/R_true^2. (4.6)\n\nIndeed substituting(4.1) at s'=S_L into(2.6) gives the displayed last\nterm. Until absorption is proved, both old terms in(4.6) are retained.\n\n## 5. A finite last-crossing absorption lemma\n\nThis is the comparison used in this round. It treats the true quadratic\ncaps, the moving interval and all large steps exactly. It is NOT a\ncontinuous-time limit or an approximation by the total word mass.\n\nConsider(4.4) on a finite recursive word H_L with positive step values\nb_0,...,b_(L-1), for fixed sigma>0, cap_ratio>0, beta>0 and A_2. Suppose\nits incoming state has w_in<A_2 and positive width g_in=A_2-beta*v_in.\nLet\n\n    P_j=sum_{h in H_j, h>=2} h,\n    z_in=beta*v_in-w_in,\n    kappa_tail=1+beta^2.                            (5.1)\n\nEmpty sums are0. Suppose the following FINITE inequalities hold:\n\n    max(z_in,0) <= kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2;          (5.2)\n    sigma*(b_j-cap_ratio*P_j)/2 <= g_in,\n                                     0<=j<L;       (5.3)\n    sigma*(cap_ratio*b_j-kappa_tail*P_j)/2 <= g_in,\n                                     0<=j<L.       (5.4)\n\nThen the actual pair has entered its flat interval by the end of H_L.\n\nProof. If the incoming point is in the interval, absorption already holds.\nOtherwise w_in<A_2 forces it to be on the left, with z_in>0. Assume for\ncontradiction that the terminal pair is active. Since a point in the closed\nflat interval is absorbing, EVERY sample, including every post-step sample,\nhas then been strictly on the left or strictly on the right. There are\nfinitely many samples, so if a side change occurs it has a last occurrence.\n\nWhile consecutive samples stay on the left, z=beta*v-w>0 evolves by\n\n    z^+=z-h*kappa_tail*min(sigma,z/kappa_tail)/2.    (5.5)\n\nIf h>=2 and z<kappa_tail*sigma, the right side is(1-h/2)z<=0,\ncontradicting staying on that side. Consequently every such high step\nhas q=sigma and decreases z by h*kappa_tail*sigma/2. This also covers\nthe cap boundary; if it would land at0 that would be absorption or a\nside change, which is excluded on a constant-side segment. Low steps\nh<2 still cause nonnegative decrease. Likewise, on a constant right-side\nsegment, t=w-A_2>0 evolves by\n\n    t^+=t-h*min(cap_ratio*sigma,t)/2,               (5.6)\n\nso every h>=2 must have r=cap_ratio*sigma and decreases t by precisely\nh*cap_ratio*sigma/2. This proves both necessary high-step mass losses\nwithout neglecting any low step or near-boundary query.\n\nIf there is no side change, the whole H_L stays left, and(5.5) gives\nz_in>kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2, contrary to(5.2).\n\nOtherwise let the last side change occur at a step of level j. Every\noccurrence of b_j in H_L is immediately followed by a full H_j. This\nfollows by induction from H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j): a node's right subtree\nis read immediately after the node, before any ancestor or later subtree.\nThat complete following H_j lies in the original remaining tail.\n\nFor a left-to-right change, at the preceding sample write z=beta*v-w>0\nand g=A_2-beta*v>=g_in. The new right margin satisfies exactly\n\n    t^+=h*q/2-z-g < b_j*sigma/2-g_in.              (5.7)\n\nBy lastness of the crossing the following H_j remains strictly right.\nEquation(5.6) therefore requires t^+>cap_ratio*sigma*P_j/2.\nCombined with(5.7) this contradicts(5.3).\n\nFor a right-to-left change, at the preceding sample put t=w-A_2>0.\nThe left coordinate v is unchanged on this step, and the new left margin\nsatisfies\n\n    z^+=h*r/2-t-g < b_j*cap_ratio*sigma/2-g_in.     (5.8)\n\nThe following H_j remains strictly left, so(5.5) requires\nz^+>kappa_tail*sigma*P_j/2, contradicting(5.4).\nAll cases contradict terminal activity. The flat interval is reached,\nwhich proves the finite lemma.\n\nThis proof is valid even if the gap increases substantially and if many\nearlier crossings occurred. It does not compare two different words of\nequal mass. The exact following-subtree property is indispensable.\n\n## 6. Uniform control of the local backward data\n\nWe now derive the bounds needed to verify ALL inequalities(5.2)--(5.4)\nuniformly over growing m. No prefix-to-infinite-composition exchange occurs.\nFor fixed beta write\n\n    I_beta=sqrt(2)(1+beta)-beta<2,\n    Y_beta=beta(exp(I_beta/beta)-1),\n    U_beta=beta+(C_beta-beta)(Y_beta+beta)/beta.     (6.1)\n\nFor the finite backward trajectory in Section2 put\nw_i=(y_i+beta)/(q_i+beta). At each uncapped point the radial equation is\nu_i-beta=(p(q_i)-beta)w_i. Taking a cross product of(2.8) with\nv(q_(i-1)) cancels the alpha_i term and gives\n\n    w_(i-1)=w_i[1+\n       (p(q_(i-1))*q_i-q_(i-1)*p(q_i))\n            /{beta*(p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1))}].         (6.2)\n\nAt a transition to q_(i-1)=0 this same formula follows from y_(i-1)=y_i;\nif both q values are0 it is equality. Thus all endpoint cases are covered.\nThe numerator is\n\n    integral_(q_(i-1))^(q_i)\n                 [p(q_(i-1))-q_(i-1)*p'(t)] dt.\n\nAfter dividing by p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1), the integrand is a weighted mean\nof1 and -p'(t), hence is at most max(1,-p'(t)). Multiplying(6.2), using\n1+x<=exp(x) and w_N=1, gives\n\n    y+beta <= (q_0+beta)\n       exp(beta^(-1) integral_(q_0)^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt)\n       <= beta exp(beta^(-1) integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt). (6.3)\n\nFor the second inequality, log(1+q_0/beta)<=q_0/beta and the integrand\nis at least1. The derivative is\np'(t)=-(t+beta)/(p(t)-beta). It crosses -1 at\n(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta. Integrating on the two sides yields exactly I_beta.\nIn particular\n\n    1<=y<=Y_beta<=beta*exp(2/beta).                 (6.4)\n\nFor any q_i>0, the radial relation and y_i<=Y_beta imply u_i<=U_beta.\nThe steps with q_(i-1)>0 form one contiguous final part of the ordinary\nblock. Their total sum of alpha_i p(q_(i-1)) is at most U_beta-beta,\nby telescoping u_i. Since p>=beta and C_beta<2,\n\n    u=beta+C_beta*S/2-E_gap,\n    0<=E_gap<=C_beta*(U_beta-beta)/beta\n                       <=4 beta^(-1) exp(2/beta).  (6.5)\n\nAll other steps have q_(i-1)=0 and p=C_beta, so they contribute no gap\nin(6.5). Define the explicit bound\n\n    K_bound(beta)=6 beta^(-1) exp(2/beta).          (6.6)\n\nCombining M=u+y/beta with(6.4)--(6.5) proves\n\n    |M-C_beta*S/2|<=K_bound(beta),\n    |u-C_beta*S/2|<=K_bound(beta),\n    y/beta<=exp(2/beta).                           (6.7)\n\nThese are estimates for arbitrary ordered finite ordinary blocks. They\nhave been derived jointly, rather than borrowed from a source exponent\nor from the earlier unverified draft.\n\nThey suffice uniformly for the moving beta range in Section1. Since\nbeta^(-1)exp(2/beta) decreases with beta, beta>=8/m implies\n\n    K_bound(beta)<=(3m/4)exp(m/4)<=m(4/3)^m.        (6.8)\n\nThe last estimate follows from exp(1/4)<sum_(r>=0)(1/4)^r=4/3.\nIt is a symbolic exponential bound, not a numerical evaluation.\n\nFor the actual word put v_mass=a_m/(c_m-2). Since\nc_m(c_m-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>eta_m, one has0<v_mass<1. The finite mass\nidentity, proved by substitution into S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j and S_0=0, is\n\n    1+S_j=v_mass*c_m^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j.              (6.9)\n\nAs m grows, v_mass->1 and c_m->lambda uniformly for tau in[1/5,1/4].\nThe identity m log(1+tau/m)->tau is uniform there; for example integrating\n1/(1+x) gives 0<=tau-m log(1+tau/m)<=tau^2/(2m).\nConsequently, for J=m-2 and L=m-1,\n\n    S_J/lambda^J -> exp(tau),\n    S_L/lambda^L -> exp(tau)                       (6.10)\n\nuniformly in tau. In particular S is bounded below by a positive constant\ntimes lambda^m for all sufficiently large m. Because4/3<2<lambda,\n(6.8)--(6.10) imply, for EVERY fixed nonnegative integer r,\n\n    m^r(1+2^J+K_bound(beta))/S ->0                 (6.11)\n\nuniformly on the rectangle. Polynomial factors times a fixed geometric\nratio below1 tend to0; this follows, for instance, by taking the ratio of\nsuccessive terms and bounding it eventually by a constant below1.\nEquation(6.11) is the full uniform error estimate used below.\n\n## 7. Every crossing level satisfies the finite comparison\n\nWrite d_m=c_m-2. Formula(6.9) gives the exact identity\n\n    b_j-d_m*S_j=c_m-1-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^j\n                 <=c_m-1.                         (7.1)\n\nThe sum P_j in(5.1) obeys\n\n    0<=S_j-P_j<=2(2^j-1)<=2^(j+1),                (7.2)\n\nbecause each discarded step is below2 and the word has2^j-1 entries.\nThis is an inequality used within the last-crossing proof, not a claim\nthat the actual tail depends only on its mass.\n\nSet epsilon_M=M-C_beta*S/2, so |epsilon_M|<=K_bound(beta). From the\nEXACT cap_ratio=C_beta*(b_J-2)/(2M) and(7.1),\n\n    cap_ratio-d_m\n      ={ C_beta[(c_m-3)-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^J]\n                              -2d_m*epsilon_M }/(2M).       (7.3)\n\nFor all sufficiently large m, 2<c_m<3, 0<d_m<1 and1<C_beta<2. Therefore\n\n    |cap_ratio-d_m|<=Xi_m,\n    Xi_m=(1+2^J+K_bound(beta))/M.                   (7.4)\n\nBy(6.7) and(6.11), M>=S/4 eventually, uniformly. Hence S/M<=4 and\nm^r Xi_m->0 for every fixed r. In particular cap_ratio->d uniformly,\nand cap_ratio*d_m<kappa_tail eventually, since d^2<1<=kappa_tail.\nWe may also use cap_ratio<=2 and kappa_tail<=17/16<2 uniformly.\n\nFor EVERY j with0<=j<=J=L-1, (7.1)--(7.4) imply\n\n    sigma*(b_j-cap_ratio*P_j)/2\n      <=[|d_m-cap_ratio| S+c_m-1+2cap_ratio*2^J]/(2M)\n      <=4 Xi_m.                                   (7.5)\n\nIndeed the first term is at most2Xi_m using S/M<=4, and the other\nterms are at most(1+2*2^J)/M<=2Xi_m. Likewise, using\ncap_ratio*d_m-kappa_tail<0,\n\n    sigma*(cap_ratio*b_j-kappa_tail*P_j)/2\n      <=[cap_ratio(c_m-1)+2kappa_tail*2^J]/(2M)\n      <=2 Xi_m.                                   (7.6)\n\nThese estimates cover all levels simultaneously, including small levels\nwhose steps could never cross the positive-width interval in the first\nplace. No large-bridge transition is left to a pointwise limiting map.\n\nWe now compare them to the ACTUAL incoming gap. Equations(6.7),(6.9)\nand(6.11) give the following uniform asymptotics, where the difference\nfrom each displayed beta-dependent expression tends to0:\n\n    D_2 - d/C_beta ->0,\n    D_3 - d/C_beta^2 ->0,\n    A_1 ->0, ell_2 ->0,\n    R_true^2 ->1.                                  (7.7)\n\nFor D_3, use b_(J+1)=c_m*b_J-(c_m-1), so T_2/S->1/2 uniformly, while\nT_1/S->d/2 and M/S-C_beta/2->0. For A_1 use the stronger bound\ny/(beta*M)<=exp(2/beta)/M from(6.7)--(6.8); this tends to0 uniformly.\nThe radius convergence follows from its EXACT identity(3.7).\nSince C_beta<2, eventually D_2>=d/4 uniformly. Consequently(4.5) gives\n\n    g_in>=beta^2 D_2>=16d/m^2.                     (7.8)\n\nTogether with m^2 Xi_m->0, (7.5)--(7.8) establish(5.3)--(5.4) uniformly\nfor every level of every sufficiently large word.\n\nIt remains to check the incoming condition(5.2). The incoming right\nmargin is w_in-A_2=-beta D_3<0. The incoming left margin is exactly\n\n    z_in=beta D_3-beta^2 D_2-ell_3/beta\n              <=beta D_3.                        (7.9)\n\nBy(7.7), D_3<=1 eventually, so max(z_in,0)<=1/4 uniformly. Meanwhile\n(6.10),(7.2) and(6.7) yield\n\n    kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2\n          - kappa_tail*lambda/C_beta ->0           (7.10)\n\nuniformly. The second expression is larger than lambda/2>1 for all our\nbeta. The left side of(5.2)'s bound is thus at most1/4 while its right\nside exceeds1 for all sufficiently large m. This proves(5.2).\nIf the incoming point were already flat, no comparison would be needed;\notherwise all hypotheses of the finite last-crossing lemma now hold.\nThe two retained components therefore vanish at the ACTUAL final state.\nThis proves(1.3), with every physical step accounted for.\n\nThe estimates explicitly pay the width scale of order m^(-2), the full\nexponentially growing word, the small-step mass omitted only from a lower\nbound, every possible last-crossing level, and all quadratic cap queries.\nThere is no unproved interchange of a fixed-prefix limit with a growing\ncomposition. No comparison repair or change of the function was used.\n\n## 8. Original benchmark normalization and the genuine deficit\n\nRetain the original all-split definitions\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                  (8.1)\n\nNo denominator is recomputed or replaced. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750\nstates that Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m) B_(2^m-1) exists and is positive and\nfinite. Its enclosure at b=1,j=0 gives\n\n    Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=(203/100)^2<21/5,      (8.2)\n\nwhere B_1=G(1,1)=4 follows directly from(8.1). This is the only accepted\ninput used beyond elementary statements proved jointly in this report.\n\nFrom(6.10), uniformly in tau,\n\n    B_(n_m)/(1+S_L) -> Z_1*lambda*exp(-tau).        (8.3)\n\nSubstitute(7.7), the EXACT zero retained values, and R_true^2->1 into\n(1.3). Since d=1/lambda, (8.3) proves exactly the uniform approximation\n(1.4). For a fixed beta>0 the moving rectangle includes it once m>=8/beta;\nthis proves(1.5). For beta_m=8/m, C_beta tends to1. The function C_beta\nis strictly increasing in beta, so the uniform approximation also proves\nthe supremum limits in(1.6) and over the full tau rectangle. This uses\nuniform control, not an exchange of an uncontrolled supremum and limit.\n\nFor explicit benchmark margins, lambda>12/5 because sqrt(2)>7/5, and\nexp(1/4)>1+1/4 gives exp(-1/4)<4/5. Thus\n\n    Z_1*exp(-1/4)/(2lambda)\n          < (21/5)(4/5)/(24/5)=7/10.              (8.4)\n\nSimilarly exp(-1/5)<5/6 gives\n\n    Z_1*exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)\n          < (21/5)(5/6)/(24/5)=35/48<3/4.        (8.5)\n\nThe strict limiting margins and uniform convergence imply the stated\neventual finite-horizon deficits. These are symbolic inequalities, with\nno saved benchmark arithmetic replay, numerical evaluation or new DP.\n\nThere is no conflict with the positive post-root retained value in the\nsource candidate. For fixed beta, (3.4) and(7.7) give\nz_in->beta*d*(1-beta*C_beta)/C_beta^2>0. The finite tail proved above\nerases that value exactly. The equal-mass tail counterexample from the\nsource candidate also remains intact: the present argument uses the\nspecific recursively nested following blocks, not a mass-only law.\n\n## 9. Source provenance, residual scope and resource handoff\n\nThe unchanged physical construction originated in the direct convex\nreconstruction of the shifted circular mechanism in Kaizhao Liu and\nYuhan Ye, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent: The Strongly\nConvex Case, arXiv:2609.26873v1, https://arxiv.org/html/2609.26873v1 .\nIts source role and the completed earlier reading are recorded in the\nfrozen joint candidate4c4a21a484564bdc, especially Sections2--6 and9--10.\nThis report reconstructs all needed convex ingredients directly. Geometry, chronology, and true radius\n\nPut theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon) and\n\n    C=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp),\n    R(theta)=C*exp(-kappa*theta),\n    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),\n    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),\n    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)).\n\nThe supporting normal is n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa*t(theta). For\nDelta=phi-theta, the derivative of\nexp(-kappa*Delta)(cos Delta+kappa*sin Delta) is\n-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa*Delta)sin Delta. It changes sign in the correct\ndirection at0 for the entire permitted angle interval. This validates\nsupport against both earlier and later arc points, not merely adjacent\nsampled slopes. Support against0 is immediate from its smaller value.\n\nFor one local chronological ordinary list, the backward endpoint is\n(epsilon,-1). Elementary inequality(25/31)x^(2p)+(1-x)^p<=1 forp>=5/8 proves the dominant envelope without any recurrence evaluation, saved endpoint or numerical program.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED auxiliary result inside joint draft34bf5f19be16ad26; no fact_id and no fact_submit allocation. Define lambda=1+sqrt(2),rho=log_2(lambda), C_0=B_0=1, Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2, G(a,b)=2a+b/2+sqrt(b^2+8ab)/2, C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)), B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)) for every integer n>=1, retaining every integer split. The sole accepted input for the ray corollary is04219554e41d9750: Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1) exists and is positive finite. The all-n power bounds below are rederived, not read from an unverified saved-endpoint transcription. This is scalar benchmark control only, not a GD certificate or target theorem.\n\n## 9. An elementary all-split scalar envelope and absolute deficit\n\nThis section is part of the same JOINT UNVERIFIED argument. Its\nscalar bound is proved independently of saved numerical endpoints.\nSet rho=log_2(lambda), p=rho/2 and kappa=62/25. One has p>5/8,\nbecause lambda^4=17+12sqrt(2)>32 (use sqrt(2)>5/4).\n\nFirst the following elementary power inequality holds:\n\n    (25/31)x^(2p)+(1-x)^p<=1,\n                0<=x<=1, p>=5/8.                   (9.1)\n\nIncreasing p can only decrease the left side, so consider\n\n    psi(x)=(25/31)x^(5/4)+(1-x)^(5/8).\n\nIts endpoint values are 1 and 25/31. An interior critical point obeys\n\n    x^(1/4)(1-x)^(3/8)=31/50,\n    x^2(1-x)^3=(31/50)^8.                           (9.2)\n\nThe function x^2(1-x)^3 decreases for x>=2/5. For x>=5/8,\n\n    x^2(1-x)^3<=675/32768<21/1000\n        <(147/1000)^2<(31/50)^8.                    (9.3)\n\nThe fixed rational comparisons use 675000<688128,\n21000<21609, and\n(31/50)^4=923521/6250000>147/1000 since 923521>918750.\nThus every interior critical point lies below 5/8.\n\nAt a critical point, the derivative equation makes\n\n    psi(x)=(1-x/2)/(1-x)^(3/8)=:g(x).\n\nThe logarithmic derivative of g has the sign of 5x-2:\n\n    g'(x)/g(x)=(5x-2)/[8(1-x)(2-x)].\n\nConsequently its maximum on [0,5/8] is at an endpoint. Now g(0)=1\nand g(5/8)<1, the latter being equivalent to\n\n    11^8=214358881<226492416=27*2^23.\n\nThe continuous function psi attains a maximum either at an endpoint\nor at such an interior critical point. This proves (9.1).\nAll these are displayed symbolic rational inequalities, not results\nof an executed calculation or a numerical evaluation.\n\nWe also give the primitive power inequality rather than assume a\nnew recurrence fact. For positive a,b let A=a^(1/rho), D=b^(1/rho),\nT=(A+D)^rho. Put E(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. The function\nz -> log E(exp z) is convex: its second derivative is\n\n    rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)\n       *[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/E(t)^2 >=0,  t=exp z,\n\nbecause the bracket vanishes at zero and has positive derivative\nfor t>0. Convexity at opposite arguments gives\n\n    E(t)E(1/t)>=E(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2.\n\nIt follows that (T-a)(T-b)>=2ab, or\nT^2-(a+b)T-ab>=0. Since T is positive, it lies above the positive\nroot Omega(a,b). Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction over\nALL original primitive splits therefore proves\n\n    C_n<=(n+1)^rho.                                 (9.4)\n\nWe claim the corresponding dominant bound\n\n    B_n<=kappa(n+1)^rho,              n>=0.          (9.5)\n\nThe case n=0 is 1<=kappa. In an arbitrary split at n>=1 put\nA=k+1, D=n-k and N=A+D=n+1. By induction and (9.4), the two\narguments of G are at most A^rho and kappa D^rho. The function G\nis increasing in both nonnegative arguments, directly from its formula.\nIts value G(a,b) is the square of the positive root v of\nv^2-sqrt(b)v-2a=0. Therefore it is at most T=kappa N^rho if\n\n    2A^rho+kappa D^(rho/2)N^(rho/2)<=kappa N^rho.\n\nOn dividing by kappa N^rho, this is exactly (9.1) with x=A/N\nand p=rho/2, since 2/kappa=25/31. It holds for EVERY split, so\ntaking their original maximum proves (9.5), without balancing or\ndeleting any split. In particular, (1.1) gives\n\n    Z_1<=62/25.                                    (9.6)\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"joint_finding": "34bf5f19be16ad26", "fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "artifact": "/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/noncore_rank_body_20261001/max64_noncore_binary_staircase.md", "section": 9, "status": "unverified_no_fact", "formal_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"rho": "log_2(1+sqrt(2)), the original primitive exponent.", "kappa": "62/25, the proposed scalar all-split power-envelope coefficient.", "p": "rho/2 in the recurrence application; arbitrary real at least5/8 in the auxiliary scalar inequality.", "psi": "Function(25/31)x^(5/4)+(1-x)^(5/8) on[0,1]."}}
